# Decisions

A dated log of what was decided and why. Newest first. Open questions are in `docs/ux-spec.md` section 11.1 (UX) and in each plan's own open questions.

## 2026-10-29 (Amazing Grace stays as recorded)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Amazing Grace's arrangement stays fixed as recorded** (version 3): its notes change only for a real problem (a wrong note, an unsingable leap, a clear voice-leading error), never for "could be better". A careful review at 2, 3 and 4 singers (the arranger and an independent choral review) found none: the tune is New Britain note for note, no parallels, the duet and trio have the third on every chord change, no leap over a fifth. Taste notes, left alone: the tenor holds G3 most of the song; at the last cadence the tenor's seventh (C4) falls to G3 and the alto's leading tone to B3, which keeps the duet's last chord a third; the duet's "now" (beat 40) is a bare fourth for two beats. | Hillel: "the only song we've hired singers for is Amazing Grace, unless there are real problems with the harmony we should probably keep it fixed now". Every changed part means re-recording a paid singer. |

## 2026-10-29 (Hum a tune)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Hum a tune's musical side** (arranger, `docs/hum-a-tune-plan.md`): a new `hum.js` finds the tempo, a beat that may drift, the metre (4/4, 3/4, 6/8), the pickup and the phrases from a take hummed in free time, then cleans the rhythm (simple rhythms first) and the pitch (the Check step's fixes, notes outside the key flagged, never moved silently); the harmony is the arranger with the library's 2-to-4-singer rules. Files agreed with the frontend: `hum.js` the arranger's; a new free-time export in `transcribe.js` (`transcribe()` unchanged) and the `arrange.js` rules written by the arranger, reviewed by the frontend; the screens the frontend's. | The launch includes Hum a tune (Hillel); most pieces exist, the musical core is free time. |
| **Hum in free time, with "Use a click" one tap away** and Tap along when the app isn't sure of the beat; **a wordless tune sings "mm"**, a true hum; **up to 32 bars or a minute, in one take**, with Again for any line. | Hillel: "Free time, click optional"; "mm" (not "loo"); "32 bars or a minute". |

## 2026-10-29 (every song for 2, 3 and 4 singers)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Every song is made complete with 2, 3 and 4 singers by rewriting its alto and tenor, with no change to the note format** (arranger, `docs/arrangements-voicings-plan.md`): when the tune doesn't have the chord's third the alto does, the tenor becomes a moving line rather than a drone, the alto crosses above a low tune only briefly, and the tune and bass stay as they are. 17 songs change, a few per landing, each listing the parts that changed and going up a version. Amazing Grace (its one real voice) and the two rounds stay as they are; Silent Night gets one alto fix (a major-seventh leap). No duet-only or trio-only lines (`variantOf` parts or cue notes). | Hillel: "Yes". The duets and trios lost the third wherever only the tenor had it (in the median song about 26% of the trio and 28% of the duet). A choral review agreed and found no song that needs its own line, which would add guides to record and pay for and change every parts list. |
| **Wade in the Water's duet is soprano and alto** (was soprano and bass), and **Michael Row moves from D to F**, every part, so its alto has room for the third under the tune. | Hillel: yes to both. The groove duet had no third for 61% of the song; in D the alto had no room under Michael Row's low D4. |

## 2026-10-29 (two skins)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The site has the app's two skins** (`docs/skins-plan.md`): `<html data-skin="game|serious">`, set before paint by theme-boot.js from the same stored choice as the app's, with "Game mode" on or off beside the site's theme switch. Serious is the site as it is; game is the arcade mockup's look (v2): its palette with AA text colours, Bungee headlines, Press Start 2P only for small HUD labels, Figtree for reading, flat buttons, gloss only on note gems, scanlines only on screens. Help, legal and guide articles stay calm in both. Only labels and the game features' words differ by skin; the facts never do. The default (and any first-run choice) is one setting in theme-boot, still Hillel's call. | Hillel: "two skins for one app", "the entire system should work in either game mode or serious mode", "it can be part of the theme toggle: game mode on/off". |
| **Two new fonts: Bungee and Press Start 2P,** from Google Fonts like Figtree, one weight each, `display=swap`. | Hillel: "Yes, add both". Bungee gives the game skin's marquee headlines; Press Start 2P its HUD labels; both small, and the CSP already allows Google Fonts. |

|---|---|

## 2026-10-29 (the landing's hero)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The landing's hero keeps "Harmony, made by you." and takes the reference's text and layout:** under it, "Learn a part, sing it over the others, and share a song where every voice is yours. Bring friends or your choir, too. It runs in your browser and it's free to start." on the left, with "Start singing, it's free" and "Hear four voices" on the right, centred on it (stacked full width on a phone). "Hear four voices" brings the hero's four-voice player into view and plays it. This replaces the 27 October line "For one voice, or a whole choir." under the headline (it stays in the page's description) and the hero's single call to action. | Hillel: "keep our style but use their text and layout". |

## 2026-10-28 (the arranger's checks kept)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The arranger's checks are a command, `app:templates:check`, and a test**, not a throwaway script: hard errors (overlapping or misaligned parts, a pitch outside a voice's hard range, a phrase without words) fail the build; ranges against the amateur ones (S C4-E5, A G3-C5, T C3-G4, B F2-D4, the review's floors and ceilings), leaps over a sixth, parallel fifths and octaves (passing notes allowed), long notes off the `chords`, missing thirds in the 2- and 3-singer voicings, and the bar arithmetic are advice. A round skips the parallels and thirds. No template was changed: Hillel hasn't approved the fixes. | So no later edit brings a problem back, and the advice count shows the approved fixes landing. |

## 2026-10-28 (My parts and What's next)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **My parts gets what the frontend's screen needed** (agreed with the frontend): a take's `adminNoteAt` (when Hillel wrote his note, from when he decided) and `url` (their own take to hear again, signed for 15 minutes, once processed). | "Hillel · 2 days ago" dates the note, and a retake card lets them hear what they sent. |
| **No "Email me when it opens" for beta singers.** The beta plan's "No automatic email" stands (the coordinator checked: Hillel hasn't changed it); a song not yet reviewed shows that in My parts, and opens for recording when it's reviewed. | A product rule only Hillel changes. |
| **What's next's "also learned" names a song only when at least 5 of the song's finishers finished it too** (`templates.also_learned_min`), on top of the finishers' own floor of 20. | So the list never tells what one or two people did. |

## 2026-10-28 (the old "13 or over" dropped)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **When you were born (month and year) is the only age answer** (Hillel: "drop old 13+ answer"). Sign-up needs it; sign-up and `account.update` refuse `ageConfirmed` (`422`); the account no longer carries `ageConfirmed` (the clients read `birthGiven`). An account that ticked only the old box keeps working as before, and the clients ask its month and year once. `age_confirmed_at` stays: it now comes from the birth date, and on older accounts from the old answer until they give one. Sequenced so nothing in use broke: the web (957ba095) and the app (71bcc8d) stopped sending and reading it first. | A tick box let someone refused as too young just tick it; a birth date can't be dodged that way, and makes 18 automatic. |

## 2026-10-28 (the pre-launch security review)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Email addresses compare byte for byte** (`utf8mb4_bin` on every email column), and account mail goes to the account's own address, never the one typed. | The default collation ignores accents: a look-alike address found someone's account and got their reset or sign-in link (the review's one high finding). |
| **Wrong passwords count per address and network (10 in 15 minutes) and per address everywhere (50 an hour)**; the limiters key on the network's HMAC without the date. | One network could keep an owner out; limits started again at midnight. |
| **The caps on account emails per address stay**, though a stranger can use them up for a while. | They're what stops us being used to flood an inbox; the owner can still sign in by password or provider. |
| **Uploads are read only as WAV and only from local files; names and subjects stay on one line; a far-off choir voice is left out of the mix; the purge never takes a file still being processed; every admin change is audited.** | The review's other findings ([security-review.md](security-review.md)). |

## 2026-10-28 (the numbers gone from peaks)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Every take carries `peaks8`; `peaks` as numbers is gone**, and `?peaks=8` is still accepted. A sync page holds up to 1,000,000 peaks. | The web (38f971f9) and the app (9fda19b) both read `peaks8` first; the drop ships with the app's push. |

## 2026-10-28 (compact peaks)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **`?peaks=8` on `sync.pull`, `projects.show` and `takes.show` sends a take's peaks as `peaks8`** (base64, one byte each, clamped, times 255, rounded half away from zero; `peaks` is then `null`), and a compact sync page holds four times the peaks. Without it, nothing changes; when the web and the apps both ask for it, `peaks` as numbers goes. Agreed with the frontend and mobile; the contract has the rounding and a worked example so all three agree byte for byte. | A first sync of 150 songs fell from 80 MB in 55 pages to 19 MB in 14 (measured locally). |
| **Peaks only come down.** No request takes a take's peaks: the server makes them from the uploaded WAV. | Mobile asked to send peaks8 up; nothing reads what the app sends, so it needs no change (and can stop sending them). |

## 2026-10-28 (anonymous reads without a session)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **An anonymous API read (a GET or HEAD with no session or remember cookie and no app token) starts no session**: it runs on the array driver and its answer sets neither the session cookie nor XSRF-TOKEN. `auth.config` still starts one, and is where the app gets its CSRF cookie, at start and in its 419 recovery (agreed with the frontend). A browser with a session, the throttles and the apps are unchanged. | A client that keeps no cookies wrote a session row on every request; crawlers would have grown the sessions table without end. |

## 2026-10-28 (the local performance pass)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **A sync page ends early once its takes' peaks pass 250,000** (about 1.5 MB), counted from each take's length, and only the takes it sends have their peaks read. | With real two-minute takes a page of 200 changes was 8 MB and took seconds; the contract already lets the server end a page (`meta.more`). |
| **The gallery's newest-first list has its own index, forced** (`harmonies_gallery_index`). | MySQL sorted every ready harmony (24 KB rows) for a page of 24: 115 ms to 4 ms. The planner wouldn't choose the index itself; other tabs keep their own. |
| **Lists that don't show peaks don't read them** (`Take::WITHOUT_PEAKS`, the bin first). A test keeps the list to every other column. | The bin went from 371 ms to 51 ms for a heavy singer. |
| **Six more reads are held to query ceilings** (`QueryBudgetTest`). | So none can grow an N+1 unseen. |
| **Not changed, proposed instead:** anonymous API reads not starting a session (the frontend's CSRF flow), and peaks in a compact encoding (a contract change for the web and the apps). **For Hillel:** Valkey or Redis, with the numbers in `docs/performance.md`. | Each needs the other side, or money. |

## 2026-10-28 (the restore drill's flaky test)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The restore drill expects only rows older than 10 minutes before a backup to be in it, in both of its checks** ("came back empty" and "missing rows"); before, the first used the backup's exact second. The test now moves the clock a second between making a row and backing up, so the boundary is tested every run. | The test's row lives in its own transaction, which the dump can't see; whenever the clock ticked over between the two, the drill said the users table came back empty (reproduced every time with the tick). A real backup has the same edge: a transaction open when the dump begins lands after it. |

## 2026-10-28 (running it yourself)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **One setting makes an install self-hosted: `SELF_HOSTED=true`** (`config/selfhost.php`), off unless set. stackapella.com sets nothing and is unchanged. The plan's `IS_HOSTED` was turned around: opt-in for self-hosters, not opt-out for us. | A missing setting on our own deploy must never give everyone Plus free. |
| **Self-hosted, every account has Plus** (`Plans::plan()`, the one place that decides). The app's Plus and checkout screens aren't hidden by the server; with `plan: plus` on every account the upgrade prompts don't show, and hiding the Plus page is the frontend's. | Plus is the hosted service's business; a self-hoster's users get everything. |
| **The Cloudflare check runs only behind Cloudflare** (`BEHIND_CLOUDFLARE`: unset, on unless self-hosted). | A fresh install makes no request of its own. |
| **Self-hosted, the legal pages are the operator's own**, Markdown files in `resources/legal/local/` (ignored by git; `SELF_HOSTED_LEGAL_PATH` for another folder), and until one is written its page says it isn't, under the same title. Ours are never shown on another install. | Our drafts name Stackapella as the party; showing them on someone else's install would be wrong. |
| **Our addresses are settings** (`SITE_CONTACT_EMAIL`, `SITE_FOUNDER`, `SITE_FOUNDER_URL`, `SITE_X_URL`, `SITE_BLUESKY_URL`), defaulting to ours. The help and site words that write `contact@stackapella.com` into a sentence are the landing agent's to make placeholders. | A self-hosted install names its own. |
| **The app runs without the content folders**: the guides and what's new are read from `config/content.php`'s paths and are simply absent when the folders are; the templates sync, the catalogue, the song and guide pages, the sitemap and the demo seed all work with none. | A commercial install that may not use our content deletes it (CONTENT-LICENSE). |

## 2026-10-28 (the open-source tooling)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The public tree is made by a script, never by hand** (`server/tool/open-source/export.py`): one commit in a new local repo, 4.2's docs dropped or redacted by a rules file in which each redaction must match exactly, then the leak check on the copy. It adds no remote and pushes nothing; the org, the repos and the push stay launch-day steps for Hillel. | Hillel chose one commit in new public repos (decision 1). A script makes it the same each time, and a redaction that no longer matches stops it rather than publishing the words. |
| **`abuse-test.md` leaves the public tree** with the rest of 4.2's list. | The plan leaned to dropping it, and Hillel accepted every recommendation as written. |
| **The `for_lawyer` notes come out only with `--strip-lawyer-notes`.** | They're the lawyer's questions: they stay until the lawyer has them. |
| **`LICENSE.asc` is a step for Hillel** (`gpg --clearsign`): the export lists it as missing until it's there. | Only Hillel's key can sign it (decision 5). |
| **The README's "Running it yourself" says what's true today**, including what isn't yet (every account Plus on your own install, the Cloudflare check off by default, your own legal pages), not what the release will make true. | The README is read now; section 5.5's code isn't built. |

## 2026-10-28 (the showreel's upload)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **`app:showreel:upload` puts each render of the film in its own folder on the media CDN**, `showreel/<version>/` (the start of its manifest's hash), each file with its type and cached for a year; on the CDN the landing asks for `<SHOWREEL_URL>/<version>/<file>`, locally still `?v=<version>`. It refuses a folder whose manifest isn't the one in git, and any file that doesn't match its size and sha256. | A query string alone may not reach a CDN's cache key; a folder per render means a year's cache is never stale and a page never mixes two renders. Checking against the committed manifest means the pages ask for exactly what was uploaded. |
| **`SHOWREEL_URL` is set once**, to the media CDN's `/showreel` (the command prints it), by whoever has the keys. | A new render is a new folder under it, so it never changes. |

## 2026-10-28 (fixes from the batch's correctness review)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The brushes kit is version 2**: its open hi-hat is the open-hat samples (a piece can name the controllers it's played at, here the pedal up), and each part is mixed at the level the program's controllers give it by default (Swirly's kick no longer carries the snare buzz it mutes, and its room mics sit at its own default levels). Envelope, tuning and pan controllers aren't applied, and the build says which. | Version 1 had a closed hat on the open key and muted layers at full level. Nothing was uploaded yet. |
| **The drum kit is version 2 too**: an SFZ `offset` is cut at the sample's own rate (48 kHz for Virtuosity), before resampling; five hits changed slightly. A region's own `key` beats a range it inherits, and `pitch_keycenter=sample` stops the build instead of guessing. | A build is a version: different files never share one. |
| **Only our own words make bold in emails**: a value put into them (a name, an address, a song's title) and a support reply or a notice's text keep their `*` as text, in the HTML and the plain text. The mudeo email's plain text says "Unsubscribe in one click" before its link again. | Someone's name or reply shouldn't change how the email looks, or lose characters. |
| **The slow-query log names the job** a worker was running. | "console queue:work" said nothing. |
| **The load test counts a limit's 429 and a closed feature's 404 as answers, not failures**, times only the served answers, runs only against the beta or a local server, and never follows a redirect. | The 1% failure line would have failed on the limits it's meant to measure, and a deny-list could miss a production host. |
| **Deleting an account also deletes reports on their profile, and any password reset or sign-in link waiting at their address.** `PersonalData::INDIRECT` lists every polymorphic link and email column with what happens to it when the account goes; the test fails on a new one. Ids inside JSON columns stay outside the check (sqids at most). | They're kept by address or by morph, which the foreign-key audit couldn't see. |
| **An instrument's manifest is refused if two zones share a key (or a piece's round robin) at one velocity**, or a drums zone has no `key` (null is fine). | The contract plays one zone; the check had thrown a PHP error instead of saying why. |
| **The app's own sounds are served with their audio types** (mp3, wav, ogg, m4a). | `web/sounds` was served as a download. |

## 2026-10-28 (fixes from the batch's security review)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Opening a sign-in link on an account whose address was never confirmed starts it fresh**: its password, name, birth date, age answers and carried invite go, its other sessions and devices are signed out, and the app asks the name and age again (`needsProfile`). | Someone could sign up with another person's address and a password they know; when the owner later signed in by link, they'd have been using an account the stranger could open. Sign-up already drops such a password; the link does too. |
| **A sign-in link is sent by a queued job**: the request only checks and answers. | How long the answer takes must not tell whether an address has an account. |
| **Every "I'm 18" (groups, coaching, applications, the beta agreement) gives way to a birth date that says younger**, checked again just before it's kept. | One more path (the beta agreement) and a race (a birth date given meanwhile) were open. |
| **Pushes go only to devices still signed in** (refresh token in date), not to ones idle past 90 days. | Lock-screen news shouldn't reach a device nobody's signed in on. |
| **Every push is checked before it's pushed too** (`.githooks/pre-push`: gitleaks over the commits being pushed), and the hooks and `tool/leak-check.sh` refuse a gitleaks older than 8.25. **For Hillel:** a gitleaks step in GitHub Actions would cover a clone without the hooks; it's a new dependency (an action), so it waits for his OK. | A commit made with `--no-verify` or from a clone without the hooks still can't reach GitHub from this Mac; an old gitleaks would have read the allowlist without its limit. |
| **Kept, for now: "13 or over" still works without a birth date** (the apps send it until they ask the month and year), so someone refused as too young could tick it instead; and an account already past "13 or over" that then gives a birth date under 13 is refused the answer but not closed. **For Hillel:** when the apps ask month and year, drop `ageConfirmed`; and decide what happens to an account that says, later, that it's under 13. | Both are product calls about children's accounts, not code ones. |
| **Pushes name who and what** (a singer's name, a harmony's title) as the bell does; **for Hillel and the privacy page**: say that these pass through Apple's and Google's push services. | Already in the runbook's processors; the privacy text is the lawyer's. |

## 2026-10-28 (sign in by email)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **"Accept invite" can sign in by a link sent by email** (Hillel's invite mockup; agreed with the landing agent and the frontend): `auth.link.send` always answers the same and emails a one-time link for 15 minutes; `auth.link.open` signs the address's account in, or makes it, verified by the link, with its name and "When were you born?" asked after (`needsProfile`). Where to go after is a path in the app, checked as sign-in's; a beta invite or early-access code is carried as at sign-up; a second tap says the link was used. Only the token's hash is kept, and what it carries is encrypted; links go after a day. | One step for someone who has never used the app, without a password to make, and without telling anyone whether an address has an account. |
| **The sign-in email never names who invited them**, though the invite card does. | Anyone holding a public choir link could otherwise make our emails carry a real person's name to any address. |

## 2026-10-28 (the emails' new look)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Every email has Hillel's new look** (his mockups for verify and the mudeo email): a warm page and card in Warm's light and dark colours, the mark as the four bars on a black square (drawn as table cells, no image), a teal button with white words, a small line under it (how long the link works), the link written out in a box for when the button doesn't work, a bold-lead note under a divider, and a footer outside the card saying who it was sent to and why ("Sent to ... because ... Questions? Just reply."), then the postal address. | One look for all of them, in both themes, readable in every mail app. |
| **The mudeo email's button says "Start singing"**, the landing page's one call to action, inside a "You're invited in early" box; the six points are bold-lead rows, and the videos line is a P.S. (Hillel picked it). | The same words wherever someone is asked in. |
| **Verify says "Without the link, the address is never confirmed"**, not "No account is created until the link is tapped": a sign-up makes the account at once, unconfirmed. | The email says only what's true. |

## 2026-10-28 (when you were born)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The account asks "When were you born?", month and year only** (Hillel's mockup; agreed with the frontend), in place of "I'm 13 or over" and the separate "I'm 18 or over": a birthday counts as the last day of its month, so someone is 13 or 18 only once their whole birth month has passed; under 13 is refused (`too_young`, the code alone) and nothing kept; 18 and over is an adult at once, and a 17-year-old becomes one on their own when their birth month ends (`app:accounts:ages`, daily). The date is never sent back, only `isAdult` and `birthGiven`; it's in Download my data. | Month and year are enough for 13 and 18 and keep less about people; the last day is the cautious reading. |
| **"I'm 18 or over" still counts for an account with no birth date** (older apps, and accounts made before), and never against one whose birth date says younger. | Existing accounts keep their status, and are asked month and year only where an 18+ feature needs it. |

## 2026-10-28 (Plus courses)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The courses beyond the free path are Plus, with the first lesson of every course free**, and every card and landing page says "Try the first lesson free". The list is in `docs/plus-courses-plan.md`, nine courses in each of three groups (falsetto folded into Range and registers). | Hillel: one simple rule that lets everyone try each course. |

## 2026-10-28 (part leaderboards)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Lean per-song-part leaderboards at launch, friends and global:** the best score for each song part (pitch and timing together), counted only for "On your own" or recorded takes in the song's own key at full speed with the guide muted. Friends' boards are people you're connected to (a challenge link from the share card, a song you sang together, a group); the global top list per part is opt-in and 18 and over, shown by profile name. Under-18s see and appear on friends' boards only. Admin can remove an entry. | Hillel: "lean versions of both friends and global for the launch, early people can fill it up." |

## 2026-10-28 (anonymous usage counts)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Usage, guests included, is counted anonymously on our own server**: events (lessons started and finished, songs made, takes recorded, shares, sign-ups and the like) are added to daily totals split by guest or account and by country, with no ID, cookie, fingerprint or IP kept. Admin shows totals and trends, not unique visitors or anyone's path. The old "no analytics" rule becomes "no third-party analytics and no tracking". | Hillel wants to understand overall usage, including people without an account, "all in the app itself, no GA"; he chose anonymous counts over unique-visitor hashing or per-device IDs. |

## 2026-10-28 (the apps stay closed)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Only the main repo (server, web app, API contract) opens at launch; the Flutter apps' repo stays closed source**, with an all-rights-reserved licence in place of the AAL. The site says the open code is the server and the web app. This replaces question 9 of the open-source plan. | Hillel: "we don't want the app stores filled with clone apps." |

## 2026-10-28 (note bricks)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Every note brick is drawn by `glass.js` `gemBar` on canvas**, the one source of truth: exercises, the challenge, the workout, the coach's phrase roll, the chooser and invite previews, the song builder's chords and the creator's roll and Check, as the Sing lane and the Notes view already are. The CSS `.gem` recipe stays for non-note UI (buttons, switches, discs and dots, the course path, the range keyboard's keys). | Hillel, after the notes review sheet: "I much prefer Canvas gemBar (glass.js) to CSS .gem bricks." |

## 2026-10-28 (bounces)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|

## 2026-10-27 (the export and deletion audit)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Every column that points at a person is listed in `PersonalData`**, with the export file that holds it or why it isn't exported (staff's own actions, a secret, someone else's data, bookkeeping), and `PersonalDataTest` reads the live schema: a new column pointing at people, a foreign key that would refuse a deletion, or a person's column with no foreign key fails it. 89 columns today: 53 deleted with the account, 34 anonymised, 2 (sessions, Cashier's subscriptions) removed by DeleteAccount itself. | The next table added can't quietly miss the export or outlive the account. |
| **The export gained today's records**: Plus bought in a store (never its token), the apps' devices, the bell (never who), ideas and the voices sung on them, choir voices (each recording a link), song requests and votes, song ratings, each part's kind, and the account's push settings, theme, idea emails and store Plus. | Download my data must hold everything we keep about them (2026-10-27, M7 and M8). |
| **Fixed by the audit:** the export failed for anyone who had said they're 18 or over (the date wasn't read as a date). | |
| **Kept on deletion, anonymised:** a store purchase (so restoring it on another account links it there), a song request (for the others who want it, without why they asked), payments, disputes and security events (the law and abuse), ratings given to coaches. | Each has a reason to outlive the account; none still names the person. |

## 2026-10-27 (nothing unshareable in the repos)

| **Every commit is checked for what must never be public** (asked by the coordinator for Hillel; docs/open-source-plan.md): the pre-commit hook runs gitleaks on what's staged (`.gitleaks.toml`: gitleaks' own rules, with only the generic-key rule allowed in fixture paths, so a real key stops a commit even in a test), and refuses keys, keystores, env files, store configs (`google-services.json`, `GoogleService-Info.plist`), data dumps and CSVs (the mudeo list), audio outside `web/tests/fixtures/`, and files over 5 MB. `server/tool/leak-check.sh` scans the whole history, the published tree and the fixtures' addresses before each push. Without gitleaks installed, a commit is refused. | The repos go public at launch; a leak caught after a push is already out. |
| **Fixtures use example.com, example.net and example.org, not real consumer domains**; the mudeo list's tests use our own subdomains (`singers.stackapella.com`...), because the importer rightly skips example domains as test addresses. The word list's test that gmail isn't disposable keeps gmail. | Someone may own `ana@gmail.com`. |
| **`CONTENT-LICENSE` is in the repo as the plan's draft**, all rights reserved until the lawyer reviews it. | It must be there before the content goes public (open-source plan, 3.2). |

## 2026-10-27 (push to the native apps)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The bell is pushed to the native apps through APNs and FCM, built against fakes and off until `APP_PUSH_ENABLED`** (agreed with the mobile agent, whose app side comes after its desktop polish): a device registers one token for itself (`account.devices.push.update`; a new one replaces it, signing out clears it, a token that moved to a new install leaves the old device); each new bell item is one push (a day's likes or voices push once, when the first comes); per-kind switches (`pushTypes`, all on) and quiet hours (`quietHours`, in the account's time zone) in the account; held through quiet hours, dropped after 12 hours; words made by the server in the person's language, with the web's own path to open; no badges. Pushes are kept a week. | The bell's news reaching people who don't have the app open, as the token-auth plan's "Later" had it, without a push they didn't ask for or at night. |
| **The words name who and what** (a singer's name, a harmony's or idea's title, as the bell shows them, a held title as "Untitled"), and pass through Apple's and Google's services (the runbook's processors). | A notification has to say something to be worth it; nothing more than the bell itself shows. |
| **For Hillel: the FCM package for the Android app** (firebase_messaging, or a hand-rolled FCM client, and google-services.json), the APNs key and the Firebase project. | A new dependency and accounts. |

## 2026-10-27 (real instruments built; performance and headers)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The real instruments are built by `server/tool/instruments/build.py` from the CC0 picks, into MP3s and a manifest in contract/instruments.md's format 1** (agreed with the frontend and the mobile agent): every 3rd or 4th sampled semitone, 1 to 3 velocity layers, 2 or 3 round robins for kits, each sample trimmed to 3 ms before its attack and faded, the kits' mics summed at their programs' levels, no loops. Eleven sets, about 20 MB in all (docs/instrument-build.md). | One small download per instrument, one player format for the web and the apps, nothing from npm, and nothing big fetched that isn't used. |
| **Each file's encoder delay is the MP3's own figure (its Xing/LAME header's delay plus the decoder's 529), checked against where the attack lands in a decode that keeps the priming**; players skip it when their decoder kept it (decoded length at least `frames + encoderDelay / 2`) and start each sample `onset` early on the audio clock. | Decoders differ (MediaCodec and minimp3 keep the priming, AVAudioFile trims it, browsers vary), and 25 ms of it would flam drums against the voices. |
| **The build checks every sample's pitch against its key** and stops on a sample more than 60 cents out or a whole set an octave out. It found VCSL's Kawai and VSCO 2 CE naming middle C "C3" (VCSL's TX81Z "C4"), and SFZ's default key center (60) and random round robins, all fixed before anything was kept. The organ skips the check (its 16-foot drawbar sounds an octave down); the electric bass allows 90 cents (its hardest low pluck goes sharp as played). | A set an octave off would play every song in the wrong octave, and nobody would hear it until a singer did. |
| **Jeff Learman's jRhodes and Martin HD28 wait for his reply**: `electric-piano` is VCSL's TX81Z FM piano and `nylon-guitar` FreePats' Spanish guitar, the doc's backups. When he confirms CC0, the Rhodes becomes `electric-piano` version 2 and the Martin a `guitar` set. | The licences conflict elsewhere in his repos (docs/instrument-samples.md, Risks). |
| **Each set carries a `gain`, at most 1**, levelling a typical note (-26 dBFS RMS over a second, or a kit's snare, brush tap or clap at -24 over 100 ms). | Players level instruments against each other without guessing per name; never above 1, so nothing a set holds can clip. |
| **The public read-mostly reads revalidate by ETag** (`private, no-cache`; a 304 on a match), and everything else stays `no-store`. Not cached by a shared cache: an answer can name what its viewer liked or unlocked. | A repeat read costs a 304 instead of the whole body, with no risk of one person's answer reaching another. |
| **Slow queries are logged** (300 ms for one, 2 s for a request's or job's total; the SQL only, never its values), and the heavy reads are held to query budgets by a test. | The beta's load test and real use need a way to see what's slow without logging personal data. |
| **A shared cache (managed Valkey or Redis) is for Hillel** (it costs money): with the database cache, each request's rate limiters cost about 6 queries and a row lock. Not needed for the beta; the first thing to do if its load test is over budget (docs/performance.md). | |
| **The app's `img-src` allows the private bucket** (a harmony's own card is a signed private link); the rest of the Content-Security-Policy stays as it was, now held by a test for each kind of page (docs/security-headers.md). HSTS `includeSubDomains` and preload wait for Hillel to settle the subdomains. | Today's additions (Figtree, the canvases, webp, the instruments, X and Bluesky links) fit the policy; one gap was found and closed. |

## 2026-10-27 (fixes from the auth and content reviews)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **An app's `device.replace` is honoured only with that device's own access token** (even past its hour); any other id signs in as a new device, emailed as any new one. Linking Google or Apple keeps the device's sign-in time. A valid bearer token always passes; only failing ones are counted per network. | A stolen password plus a guessed device id must not sign the real phone out silently; one phone retrying a dead token must not lock out its carrier's network. |
| **Undoing "Start over" brings back only the lessons and units opened early**; XP, practice and badges earned since stay. A second reset inside the undo window keeps the first one's copy. | The reset only ever cleared those two; an undo must not erase a week's practice or a league's XP. |
| **Our social posts run on the long worker** (a share video can take X minutes to process), wait at most 150 seconds for X, fail when X's status check fails, and show as failed if the worker stops them; Bluesky falls back to the card when it refuses the video; a harmony whose title is held is posted with its card, never its video (the video shows the title). | From the content review: a post must never sit at "posting" forever, or go out with media that isn't ready. |
| **Accepted for now:** a track's kind comes from the app's request (tracks don't name their parts, so the stored part kinds can't be matched to them; an app too old to know instruments can't make instrument parts). Part names over 40 characters aren't grandfathered (no person's templates exist yet). | Noted from the content review. |

## 2026-10-27 (Plus in the apps, after the money review)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **A store subscription follows the account its store's current record names**, superseding "A purchase is never moved between accounts" (2026-10-27, built) where they differ: Apple's current transaction (from the App Store Server API) or Google's current purchase, made with an account's token, links it to that account, even if another had it; the app's own copy of a transaction, and an older Google token a later purchase replaced, never move it. A purchase with no token stays where it's linked (`409 store_linked_elsewhere`). | A newer purchase on the same Apple ID or Google account, made in another of our accounts, is that account's to have; an old copy kept on a device must never take it back. |
| **An App Store notification is only the news**: the server reads Apple's current state for the subscription and applies that (a refund for an old renewal doesn't end the period being paid now). Without the Server API key, a notification about an earlier period than the one stored changes nothing. | From the money review: REFUND, REFUND_DECLINED and CONSUMPTION_REQUEST carry the refunded transaction, which may be an old one. |
| **Plus from a store starting or ending does what Stripe's does**: an ending (when no other Plus is left) records `plus_ended_at` with reason `canceled` and settles which projects stay editable; a start clears it; either tells the devices which projects are read-only. billing.show says `status: active` for store Plus, and `source` is the store when the web subscription is cancelled but a store one renews. | One account, one Plus: the apps and the web must see the same plan whatever sold it. |

It's built and tested against fakes, behind `SOCIAL_VIDEOS` (off), which supersedes "Our featured posts carry the share card" above once it's on. | The upload flows can't be tried without our real accounts: on after one test post on each network. |

## 2026-10-27 (Plus in the apps, after its review)

|---|---|
| **Sandbox purchases give Plus only to accounts marked as store testers** (`php artisan app:store:tester <email>`; App Review's demo account and the TestFlight testers Hillel names), narrowing "accepted everywhere" above: they're still checked and kept, but give nothing to anyone else. | A public TestFlight must never mean free Plus. App Review still works through its marked account. |
| **Apple sync needs the App Store Server API** (its key set): what the app sends only says which subscription, and Apple's answer is what counts; if Apple doesn't answer, `503`, never the app's copy. The app's copy never lifts a refund, and nothing older than the last event overwrites it. | From the reviews of the build: an old signed copy kept from before a refund must not bring Plus back. |

## 2026-10-27 (Plus in the apps, built)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The server side of Plus in the apps is built** (Hillel's option A; agreed with the mobile agent): `billing.store.sync` for purchases and restores, Apple's signed transactions checked against Apple Root CA - G3 (pinned in `server/resources/certs`) and Apple's certificate markers, Google's tokens with the Play Developer API (acknowledged by the server), App Store Server Notifications V2 at `/webhooks/app-store` and Google's real-time notifications by authenticated Pub/Sub push at `/webhooks/google-play`. One Plus per account: `store_plus_until` beside Stripe, invoices and coaches. | docs/app-purchases-plan.md. |
| **Sandbox purchases are accepted everywhere**, checked against Apple's sandbox API for their environment (and Google's test purchases likewise). | App Review buys with sandbox accounts against the production server; refusing them fails review. TestFlight testers then get Plus free, which is fine for testers. |
| **A purchase is never moved between accounts**: one made with another account's token (`appAccountToken` / `obfuscatedAccountId`), or already linked to one, answers `409 store_linked_elsewhere`. Family Sharing stays off. | Stops one subscription unlocking several accounts. A deleted account's purchase is support's to move. |

## 2026-10-27 (checkout's consumer rules)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **In the EU, EEA and UK, Checkout needs the consent to start Plus at once** (agreed with the frontend): the country is Cloudflare's (`CF-IPCountry`), and where it isn't known nothing is asked. The upgrade sheet's checkbox reads, for the lawyer to confirm: "Start Plus now. I understand that once it starts, I can't withdraw within 14 days for a refund of the time already used." The server keeps the consent with the checkout: when, the words' version (`billing.start_now_consent_version`), and the country. | The 14-day right of withdrawal ends for a digital service only with that request and acknowledgement (pricing-plan, "At checkout"). |
| **Stripe's Checkout page says that Plus renews, at what price, and how to cancel**, and for a trial the date it starts charging; Stripe's own terms checkbox waits behind `STRIPE_TERMS_CHECKBOX` until Hillel sets the terms' address in Stripe. | The auto-renewal disclosures, shown before paying. |

## 2026-10-27 (the limits review)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The plan's daily cap on uploaded bytes is built as `upload-bytes`**, in megabytes (2000 a day free, 10000 Plus) so admin can change it, counted per uploader on `takes.create` (`429 rate_limited`). | It had never been built; the storage allowance alone doesn't stop a run of record-and-delete. Megabytes because admin's limit form takes at most 100000. |
| **Plus downloads stay at 60 a day**, where the trust and safety plan says 30, until Hillel says otherwise. | Already live at 60; a singer learning several songs a day shouldn't hit it. Flagged in abuse-test.md. |

## 2026-10-27 (the apps sign in)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The native apps sign in with device tokens of our own, not Laravel Sanctum** (agreed with the mobile agent; contract/api.md, "The apps: device tokens"): an access token for an hour, a refresh token for 90 days since last used, rotated on every refresh, only their SHA-256 hashes kept, prefixed `sat_`, `srt_` and `sap_` so a leaked one is recognisable. A retired refresh token still works for a minute; after that it's taken as stolen and the device is signed out (`401 token_reused`). | Rotation and reuse detection were needed on top of Sanctum anyway, and it would be a new package; firebase/php-jwt (for checking Google's and Apple's tokens) already comes with Sign in with Apple's provider. |
| **An app request carries no cookies and gets none back, and so has no CSRF check**: any request with a bearer token, and the apps' own sign-in, refresh and app-pass routes. Cookies that come with one are dropped before the session starts. | CSRF rides on cookies a browser sends by itself; with none read and none set, there is nothing to forge. The web's sign-in routes are unchanged and keep their CSRF check. |
| **Fixed while building it: the API's CSRF check was Laravel's own, not ours**, because the api group copied the web group before ours replaced it. Ours is now registered first (a test guards it). | Found because the apps' calls would have all been 419 in production (tests skip CSRF). The web lost nothing: ours only adds the sign-in forms' `_token` way in. |
| **Shield mode in the apps: Turnstile in a small web view**, sent as `turnstileToken`, as the web does. App Attest and Play Integrity are later, and need Hillel's OK (each an SDK). | Keeps one shield for both, with no new dependency. |
| **Deleting an account revokes its Apple tokens with Apple**, from the refresh token kept (encrypted) at an app's Apple sign-in. | App Store Guideline 5.1.1(v). |
| **From the security review of the apps' tokens:** a retired refresh token gets the same pair its refresh gave during its minute, never a new one, so a replay can't take a device over and two refreshes at once agree; refresh is limited per account (60 an hour) with a per-network backstop of 1200 an hour that a carrier's shared IP won't reach; unknown bearer tokens are limited per network (30 a minute); app requests save no session; a new device emails "A new sign-in", naming its platform and never the device name sent; each Google or Apple ID token signs in once; unknown key ids fetch the providers' keys again (at most once a minute); Apple's code is kept only when it's the same person's as the identity token, and is kept when Apple is linked too. | The review found these; none changes the contract. A nonce on the ID tokens is proposed to the mobile agent (a contract change). |
| **New settings for Hillel to make:** `GOOGLE_APP_CLIENT_IDS` (the apps' OAuth client IDs in Google Cloud) and `APPLE_APP_BUNDLE_ID`. Until they're set, the apps' Google and Apple sign-ins answer `401 invalid_token`; email and password work. | The runbook's secrets table lists them. |

## 2026-10-27 (invites)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **A general invite link in admin: one shared link** Hillel can give to many people, which lets anyone holding it create an account while sign-ups are paused and gives the beta pass. No per-person links and no tracking of who used it; only Copy, Pause and Replace (a new link, the old one stops) in case it spreads too far. It sits with **the preview link, moved off the mudeo email page**, on one admin page for ways into the app. Hillel sends links himself; the app emails nothing. | Hillel's call: friends and testers need accounts before launch, not only beta singers. |

## 2026-10-27 (voices first, instruments welcome)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Stackapella leans vocal but isn't voices only: instruments are welcome in every part of the app.** Any part can be an instrument (recorded in the studio, its own name, in a template, as a backing, shared), and the words lead with singing without ever saying "voices only". Official templates stay SATB; people's own templates and projects may include instrument parts. The vision says so (`docs/vision.md`). | Hillel's call. |
| **Instrument parts in people's own templates and the creator:** free part names, each marked voice or instrument, up to 6 parts (a contract change); official templates stay SATB. **Pitch feedback per part:** off by default for an instrument, with "Show my pitch" for single-note instruments. Both built after the song builder. | Hillel's call, from the frontend's instruments sweep. |
| **The public site's coming-soon feature pages are indexed by search engines now**, not held back until each feature opens. | Hillel's call: get them ranking by launch. |
| **stackapella.com and www.stackapella.com redirect (302, temporary) to the public site** until launch. | Hillel's call: the bare domain should work. |

## 2026-10-27 (the song builder)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The creator gains a song builder** (`docs/song-builder-plan.md`): pick a chord progression or a 12-bar blues (forms, turnarounds, feels) in a key chosen for the person's voice, sing or hum over a simple backing loop, then get chorus and optional bridge suggestions that blend with the verse; and the reverse, chords mapped to a sung tune. The chords become the A, T and B parts. | Hillel's idea: help people write a song, not only transcribe one. |
| **The backing is the guide voices singing the chords on "oo", plus a simple synthesised keys sound one switch away** (no sample files, no library). | Hillel's pick: it stays a cappella and the backing turns into the parts. |
| **The song builder is free.** | Hillel's pick, like the rest of the creator. |
| **It's built right after the harmony-suggestion rework, before "Hum a tune".** | Hillel's pick: both use the same music-theory code. |
| **The real instruments are CC0 sample sets** (`docs/instrument-samples.md`): the VCSL Kawai grand; jRhodes (GM subset) and Learman's Martin HD28, pending Learman's confirmation, with backups until then; the FreePats ukulele and drawbar organ; Karoryfer Meatbass and Swagbass; Virtuosity Drums, Swirly Drums, and Karoryfer and VCSL percussion; VSCO 2 CE strings for a pad. They're converted at build time from their SFZ maps into our JSON manifest plus MP3s on our CDN, loaded per instrument (12 to 18 MB in all). No GPL, ShareAlike or NonCommercial sets. | Hillel approved; he emails Jeff Learman to confirm his two sets. |
| **Real instruments, as an option:** the improved synthesis stays the default (nothing to download), and a "Real instruments" choice downloads free sample sets (CC0 or CC-BY only, licences verified) from our CDN on first use and keeps them on the device. One instrument description format and the same files serve the web (our own small Web Audio sample player) and the apps (the native engine). No NonCommercial, GPL or ShareAlike sounds. A shortlist with licences and sizes comes to Hillel before anything is added. | Hillel's call: better sound, same solution on web and mobile, free and open. |
| **The backing is overhauled before the creator's steps:** ensemble voices that breathe and glide, a generated room reverb and stereo mix, synthesised electric piano, guitar, ukulele, pad and bass, a groove per style (comping patterns, a walking bass, light percussion, humanised), arranged with fills, a fuller chorus and a real ending, plus a style picker and a small mixer. **Still all synthesis, no sample files.** | Hillel heard the first renders: the instrument cheap, the music plain, the rhythm stiff and the voices robotic. |

## 2026-10-27 (the landing page)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The landing's headline is "Harmony, made by you."** with the line under it **"Learn harmony, write a song, or sing with others."**, replacing "Four voices. Learn them one at a time." | Hillel's call: most singers can't reach every part's range, and the app offers much more than learning the four parts. |
| **The landing's mixer shows each voice's own note bars** (its notes as pitch bars in the part's colour) instead of waveforms, and **the words are shown plainly, without notes.** | Hillel's call: each part's shape is the point; the words read better alone. |
| **The landing has no list of songs.** "Start singing" leads into the app, where the songs are; the song pages stay for links and search but aren't featured in the site's navigation for now. | Hillel's call: a short catalogue reads as a downside. |
| **A person's theme follows them everywhere:** the app, the landing and every public page, on both hosts (`app.` and the public site), through a cookie on `.stackapella.com`, and from their account once signed in. **Every front-end page wears it fully**: the help pages and user guides, pricing, legal pages, the features and "For..." pages, song pages and emails' landing pages, all styled from the same theme tokens (colour, light or dark, accent), never a fixed look of their own. | Hillel's call. |
| **The landing is direction A, "the mixer"** (a four-voice mixer in the hero, Alto marked "You", the words lighting up as they're sung), **with a final UX polish pass before it's built.** | Hillel's pick of the three directions. |
| **The landing shows every planned feature, marked "Coming soon" until it's live.** Each one is tied to its feature flag, so the label goes when the feature launches. No dates are promised. This replaces the landing agent's suggestion to hide unbuilt features. | Hillel's call: show visitors the whole product they're signing up for. |
| **The phone and desktop apps are on the coming-soon list.** | Hillel's call. |
| **Only visitors who come from mudeo see anything about mudeo** (the note and the banner): everyone else sees a landing with no mudeo mention. A mudeo arrival is a link carrying `ref=mudeo` (or the `/early/` and `/preview/` links), remembered for the visit. | Hillel's call: mudeo means nothing to new singers. |

## 2026-10-27 (native mobile apps)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Native apps in Flutter, iOS and Android first, then desktop (macOS, Windows and Linux) from the same code** (Invoice Ninja's split: Flutter for desktop and mobile; Stackapella's web stays the Vue PWA), **starting now,** in their own repository (`~/Code/stackapella-mobile`), built the way the new Invoice Ninja Flutter app (`~/Code/admin`) is: its architecture, its CLAUDE.md style and its Flutter skills. The first step is an **audio spike** proving sample-exact recording over the other parts, with measured latency, on real devices, before any screens. Replaces "Web only, no app stores for now". Payments in the apps (in-app purchases) and what app stores require for children's use are written up for Hillel before they're built. | Hillel's call: his kids love the app, and an app-store app reaches people the web can't. |
| **No Flutter web: the web stays HTML, CSS and JS (the Vue PWA); Flutter is for the phone and desktop apps.** The two share the API contract, the words (`web/locales`), the themes (`palettes.json`, the tokens), the music logic through shared test vectors (DSP, theory), and the instrument files. Revisit only if keeping two sets of screens in step becomes the main thing slowing us down after launch, and even then share more logic before moving the web. | Hillel agreed: Flutter web adds nothing to the audio (it would call the same Web Audio), adds 2 to 4 MB before first paint, renders to a canvas (password managers, find, translation, accessibility and search suffer), is weakest on iPhone Safari, and would mean rewriting a mature app. Invoice Ninja made the same split. |
| **The apps are not directed to children: rated 13+ in both stores**, with the same 13+ age rule for accounts as the web (and 18+ where the web has it), no Kids category or Families listing, and no marketing to children. Sign-up reads the stores' age signals (Apple's Declared Age Range, Google's Play Age Signals) where the law asks. | Hillel's call; keeps COPPA's verifiable-parental-consent rules out of scope. |
| **The apps' first packages are approved:** go_router, drift with sqlite3 (default unencrypted build), path_provider, and the dev-only drift_dev, build_runner and flutter_lints, all as the Invoice Ninja app uses them. **SQLite is allowed only inside the apps as the on-device store**; the server and its tests stay MySQL only. | Hillel's OK; a phone can't run MySQL, and drift gives typed queries, tested migrations and the sync outbox. |
| **The apps' audio aims for the lowest latency each device can give**, not just enough to line takes up: the fastest audio path on every platform (exclusive or MMAP modes where they exist), small buffers, and the round trip measured and shown in the spike for each device and route. A library that measurably lowers latency (such as Oboe on Android) may be proposed with its numbers. | Hillel's call: hearing yourself and the other parts with no lag is what makes it feel like singing together. |

## 2026-10-27 (your tune, sung)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **"Hum a tune, hear it harmonised, hear it sung by real people" becomes a headline use case,** highlighted on the landing page and in the app (the home's "Make your own" becomes "Hum a tune"). Step 3 (Hillel, the same day): the writer shares the tune as an **idea in a public gallery** with every part open, anyone may choose to sing it, and **if someone does, the writer is emailed** (at most one email per idea per day, an on/off setting and one-click unsubscribe: an allowed exception to "no recurring email"). Free. No admin queue. The plan and its open questions are in `docs/your-tune-plan.md`. | Hillel: "a super powerful concept". Most people with a melody can't sing or arrange it; nobody else turns a hummed idea into a real choir. |

The part colours (S A T B) stay fixed per palette. | Hillel: the swatches didn't match the themes, and he'd like people to choose an accent too. |

## 2026-10-27 (the app moves to app.stackapella.com)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The app lives at `app.stackapella.com`, starting now:** the PWA, sign-in, the admin and the API. **The public site** (the landing page, pricing, help, the song and guide pages, shared harmonies `/h/…`, the embed, legal, security.txt, sitemap) stays on `beta.stackapella.com` until the launch, then moves to `stackapella.com`. One App Platform app serves both hosts; Laravel routes by host. App routes asked for on the public host redirect to `app.` (and old installs are sent there). The landing is always the landing; the "first visit" rule goes. The coming-soon gate and the beta pass apply to `app.` before launch. | Hillel's call: the landing is always visible, the app installs once at its permanent address (no reinstall at launch), and the public pages can be cached and indexed apart from the app. Replaces "Laravel serves the PWA, the API and the public pages on one origin". |
| **Sessions are scoped to `app.stackapella.com`.** The public pages don't need the session; they link into the app ("Open the app", "Learn this harmony", "Report" posting to the app's API with CORS for that one endpoint, or Report moving into the app). | Keeps auth simple and the public pages cacheable. |

## 2026-10-27 (public songs' voices can become guides)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **When someone makes a song public, its parts may be used as guide voices for other singers,** chosen by Hillel as a version of that part (or its default), always credited by the singer's profile name. This is a condition of making a song public, said plainly at that moment ("Public songs can be featured: your parts may become the voice other singers learn from, credited to you"), in the terms of service (a licence to use public recordings as guides and in features), in the privacy policy, and in the help pages. Private songs are never used. Making a song private again, or deleting it or the account, removes its voices as guides (cached copies drop out as the CDN and devices refresh). Every singer on a shared song must agree before it goes public, as for featuring. | Hillel's call: the library grows from real voices, and people should know clearly before they publish. |

The switch has two choices, light and dark; **tapping the selected one again goes back to matching the device.** Settings' "How it looks" follows the same model. | Hillel's call: one place, one tap, and no separate "Match my device" button. |

It matches the home and Sing, and is exact to edit. |

Anyone can still sing any song in the library and Sing, which never lock. | Hillel's call: one clear way in. |

## 2026-10-26 (no browser dialogs)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Never use the browser's own dialogs** (`window.confirm`, `alert`, `prompt`), in the app, the admin or the server's pages. Every confirmation is the app's own styled modal that names exactly what will happen ("Delete 'Silent Night for Mum'? Its 3 parts go to Recently deleted for 30 days."), with the action on a clearly labelled button ("Delete project", not "OK"), a destructive style when it destroys, and Cancel. Enter confirms. A "Don't ask again" option skips that kind of confirmation from then on and does the action at once; it's offered only where the action can be undone (the bin, Undo), never for what can't be (account deletion, removing a sign-in method), and Settings can turn the questions back on. A check in `tool/test.sh` fails on any use. | Hillel's call: the browser's boxes look broken and don't say clearly what you're confirming. |
| **Sing's back arrow returns to where you came from** (the home, the studio or the lesson), not always the studio; the studio stays one tap away in Sing. | Hillel landed in the multitrack when he thought he'd stopped singing (shown on the screen map). |

|---|---|

| **The Words / Notes switch stays on screen on phones,** not in Settings. | The note lane is a beginner's best view; it shouldn't be two taps deep. |
| **Your turn on a phone is a band in your part's colour at the top,** with the lane or words and the controls still visible. Replaces the full-colour screen (decision 4 in `docs/singing-ux-plan.md`). | As in 2b; the singer keeps their place and the transport. |
| **Your voice draws as a live line while you sing, and notes light up as you hit them,** built with the redesign, not later. | The home promises "Your notes light up as you hit them", and pitch is already measured for scores. |
| **"You" is shown in your part's colour** (the Alto always olive, and so on), not one fixed colour. | It matches the studio's track colours, so the part you sing is always the same colour. |
| **Also:** hyphens on the Stage ("A-maz-ing"), chord names along the lane's top while chords are on, and the admin version badge in the bottom-right corner on phones. | The designer's smaller questions, with the recommended answers. |

## 2026-10-26 (baritone)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The template library stays SATB for now.** A baritone part is used only where the style calls for it (barbershop's tenor, lead, baritone and bass; contemporary a cappella with 5 or 6 parts), as one of that arrangement's own parts; the template format already allows any named parts with their own ranges. | Hillel's call: four parts suit beginners singing alone; baritones sing the bass (or a low tenor) in SATB. |

## 2026-10-26 (beta singers have accounts)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Beta singers sign in to the app like everyone else** (Google, Apple, or email and password), not through a private no-password link. Their credit ("Sung by ...") is their profile's name, which they change by editing their profile. Replaces the beta-singers plan's "a private link per singer with no password". The agents work out the details (how an invite still lets someone sign up while sign-ups are paused, how the singer role and the agreement attach to the account, what happens to links already sent) and log them. | Hillel's call: one way in for everyone, and singers manage their own credit. |
| **Every accepted recording of a part is a published version, and the admin picks the default.** For each part of a song, the admin chooses which singer's version learners hear by default; a learner can switch to any other published version of that part as their guide. When the default goes away (revoked, or the singer deletes their account), the part falls back to another published version the admin picks, or the synth guide. **Later, the custom choir is the same picker with more than one choice:** a learner selects several singers for a part, and they sing it together. So a learner's choice for a part is kept as a list of versions (one item today), not a single version. | Hillel's call: the best take leads, but singers' work stays available and learners get a choice. Keeping the choice as a list now means the choir needs no change to stored data. |
| **When a singer deletes their account, their recorded guides are removed from the app** (what they were paid stays on record). Mixes and exports already made from them aren't touched. | Hillel's call: removal is what someone deleting their account would expect. The voices still exist in audio people have already mixed. |

## 2026-10-26 (production hosting)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The agents may push `main` to GitHub whenever they like.** Every push deploys to the beta (`deploy_on_push`). Replaces "pushing" in the agents' "Still never" list; deploying by hand, accounts, money and the rest stay Hillel's. | Hillel's call, so he can test each milestone on the beta as it lands. |

App Platform's `nyc` region can only join NYC1 VPCs, so NYC1, not NYC3. |
| **Spaces stays in NYC3** (`stackapella-media` with the CDN, `stackapella-private`, `stackapella-backup`), with an app key limited to those three buckets. | Spaces has no NYC1. Both are in the New York area, a millisecond or two apart. |
| **The two Docker base images are OK'd** (`php:8.4-fpm-bookworm` and `composer:2`, pinned by digest). | Hillel's OK ("Needs Hillel" item 7). |
| **Cloudflare for the whole `stackapella.com` zone:** SSL Full (strict), Always Use HTTPS, TLS 1.2 or newer, Email Obfuscation off. | The zone only held email records, so nothing else was affected. Email Obfuscation rewrites pages with a script the CSP blocks. |
