Replaces "payment decided later; volunteers for now". | Hillel's call. |

## 2026-10-26 (trimming)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Basic trimming in M4:** drag a part's start and end in, "Trim the silence" in one tap, and "Line up the ends" (same end, or same start) across a project, with 10 ms fades, snapping to the beat, and Undo. Non-destructive: `trimStartFrames` and `trimEndFrames` on the part; the take stays whole and dry; everything that plays or renders honours them. | Hillel's call: people need to line up the starts and ends of their parts. |

## 2026-10-26 (the agents work without stopping)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The agents never stop to ask Hillel.** He has delegated decisions to them during the build: plans first, then the newest decisions, then the vision; "proposed" items taken as written; every decision logged under "Decided by the agents (delegated)". | Hillel: "I want to press start and come back to a finished app." |
| **Contracts are agreed between the two agents;** dependencies the plans name are approved; things only Hillel can do go on a "Needs Hillel" list and never block (fakes and test modes meanwhile); the agents draft content, marked unreviewed. | So nothing waits on him. |
| **Thorough reviews at the end of every section and milestone:** an independent `code-review` (or a fresh review subagent), plus a checklist pass, with every real finding fixed before moving on. | Hillel's call. |
| **Still never:** pushing, deploying, accounts, money, live keys, real messages to real people, secrets, npm, analytics. | Outward-facing and irreversible actions stay his. |

## Decided by the agents (delegated)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The landing's mixer brings a voice forward** (Hillel, 2026-10-27, through the coordinator): the Alto starts forward ("You"); tapping a row brings that voice forward instead, 6 dB over the others (1.2 against 0.6 each, so the four together are quieter than before and never clip), with a gain ramp of about 150 ms on the AudioContext clock, even mid-play; a voice brought forward that was off comes back on. The switch at the end of a row only turns that voice on or off. For screen readers the rows are a radio group ("Alto, your part, louder"; arrows, Home and End move it) and each switch is named ":part voice" with its on or off state, so it doesn't read "on, off". The intro says "tap a voice to bring it forward, or switch one off". | Hillel's ask: the part you'd sing should stand out, and choosing it shouldn't silence anything. |
| **The public footer is four labelled columns** (Hillel, 2026-10-27, through the coordinator): the mark, "Harmony, made by you." and our social links first; then Product (Features, Pricing, What's new, Guides), Help (Help, Write to us, Email us, Accessibility), Company (About, Press) and Legal (Terms, Privacy, Community guidelines); two columns on a phone; "© year Invoice Ninja LLC" at the bottom. The social links are `config/site.php` `social.x` and `social.bluesky` (Bluesky https://bsky.app/profile/stackapella.com; X empty until its handle is confirmed), each an icon named "Stackapella on X" or "on Bluesky", hidden while empty. Navigation links on the site (the header, the footer, the back links) are plain words underlined in the accent only on hover and keyboard focus, as the app's `link-quiet`; links inside sentences keep their underline (WCAG 1.4.1). | Hillel's ask: a clearer footer, and no always-on underlines. |
| **The public site's actions are buttons, chips or quiet words, never underlined** (landing agent, from Hillel's "underlined links in the app are ugly"): "See all", "Everything it does", "More help", "Compare the plans", Contact us and the press downloads are secondary buttons; the currencies are chips; Report is a ghost button; the header and footer links are plain words with a hover tint; credits and the mudeo banner's link are quiet links (underlined only on hover). Underlines stay only for links inside sentences: the footer's contact line, the press contact, the mudeo letter, the founder's name. | The same rule as the app's (docs/ux-spec.md 4.2), so the site and the app read as one. |
| **The feature and "for" pages as built** (landing agent, docs/public-site-plan.md): `/features`, a page per feature and `/for/<who>`, one template (`views/site/page.blade.php`), their words in `lang/en/site.php`, each claim checked against the app's code and strings, not the plans (what isn't built says "will" or "coming", and the coaches page invites coaches to write in until coaching opens, as there's no application screen yet). Learn a part and the studio play the mixer; Features joins the header and footer; all of them are in the sitemap. On the way's items link to their feature pages. | A plan isn't proof something exists; one template keeps 23 pages consistent and in every theme. |
| **The landing's review, fixed** (landing agent, from an independent review): a player whose voices are still rendering can be stopped, and starting another cancels it, so two never sound together; on iPhone the players sound with the silent switch on (`navigator.audioSession`); the site's CSS uses logical properties for right-to-left; Play shows only once the script is ready; nothing is read twice by screen readers; the link card's picture exists (`web/icons/og-landing.png`, made from the real notes) and a test checks it. | Each was a real failure the reviewer showed step by step. |
| **The landing as built, direction A (board-a2)** (landing agent): the hero plays the song's first line in the app's own guide voice, rendered in the browser from the template's notes (`js/public/mixer.js` with `guides.js`), so there are no audio files; everything starts on the AudioContext clock and each voice is a gain switched with a ramp. "Got a tune in your head?" keeps its label until both Hum a tune (`site.features.your-tune.live`) and Ideas (the `ideas` flag) are open; choir groups show as a Plus feature (built and on); coaching follows its `coaching` flag and only "Find a coach" (the directory) is in On the way. `?ref=` is carried into every link to the app. | The app's own voice is what learners hear first, nothing is downloaded, and nothing claims a feature before it opens. |
| **The public site's styles are one `s-` block at the end of `web/css/input.css`** (between `landing:start` and `landing:end`, agreed with the frontend), theme tokens only, so every page follows the person's theme, light or dark, colour and accent; the drawings and icons are markup the server makes from numbers (`App\Support\Site\Drawings`, `Icons`, returned as `HtmlString`), never raw output in a view. `web/tests/flows/site_integration.py` checks every public page in all ten themes at 320 to 1920 wide. | No page keeps a look of its own (Hillel), and nothing here can change the app's styles. |
| **How the landing knows someone came from mudeo** (landing agent with the backend, from Hillel's "only people who come from mudeo see any mudeo info"): `?ref=mudeo` on the landing, or the early-access or preview link, sets `sa_mudeo=1`, a session cookie on `hosts.pass_domain` (both hosts, like the beta pass), unencrypted and holding no one (`App\Support\Pages\MudeoArrival`). The landing reads it (it's already `no-cache`, `Vary: Cookie`), shows the banner and the letter only then, and carries `ref=mudeo` into its links to the app, which records it at sign-up. | The email links to `/early/<code>` on the app's host, so the landing would otherwise never know; a session cookie forgets them when the browser closes, and nothing about the person is stored. |
| **From M3, anyone who signs in with Google or Apple gets an account** (backend, M3), and admin rights still come only from `app:admin:grant` plus the three checks; signing in at `/admin` leads to the second factor only for admins and editors. Replaces M1's "only the people on the lists". | Accounts open in M3, with their protections built. |
| **Every way of signing in refuses a suspended account with `403 suspended`** (backend, M3): Google, Apple, verify, reset and the password, which says so only after the right password. A suspended account gets no reset link. | One rule in one place (the sign-in session), and a suspended person learns why, without telling anyone else whether an address has an account. |
| **"This wasn't me" brings the old address back for 7 days** (backend, M3; `account.email.revert`, `/email/revert?token=`): every session and "remember me" ends. It wins over an unverified sign-up made with the old address meanwhile (whose address is cleared), and refuses (`409 email_taken`) when someone has verified it. A refusal leaves the link usable. | Whoever took the account could otherwise block the undo by signing up with the victim's address. |
| **A changed handle is held for its owner for 30 days** (backend, M3): nobody else can take it, and the owner can take it back; only the latest one is held, so changing again and again can't hoard names; a deleted account's handle is held for good. | Old links and mentions mustn't pass to an impersonator the moment a handle changes. |
| **Removing a sign-in method needs a recent sign-in** (backend, M3), like changing the email or password. | Otherwise a borrowed session could remove Google and lock the owner out. |
| **Following the reset link has its own limit** (backend, M3; `password-reset-link`, 10 a minute per IP), while asking for a link stays 3 an hour per address. Sign in with Google or Apple has `provider-sign-in` (20 a minute per IP), renamed from M1's `admin-sign-in`. | Typing a weak password twice mustn't lock anyone out for an hour, and the token can't be guessed. |
| **Avatars become a 256 px square JPEG made with GD**, from a JPEG, PNG or WebP up to 2 MB and 3000 px a side (backend, M3). | No new library; the pixel cap stops a small file decoding into a huge image. |
| **An account is signed out before its row is deleted** (backend, M3). | Signing out saves a new remember token, which brought the deleted row back (found by the tests). |
| **The template sync compares music from the stored data** when a version's stored hash came from an older way of working it out, and then rewrites the hash (backend, M3 week; the first deploy's whole-file hashes). | The production deploy after M2 failed for all 19 templates although no note had changed. |
| **Singers don't see their minutes either, and the admin sees who did well** (Hillel, through his session, with "the first beta singers": about 10, the best hired for more songs): `minutes` leaves My parts (the admin and the singer's own export keep them), and the no-money test also refuses any "minute" key; the Singers screen gets each singer's `stats` (takes, accepted, retakes, published, defaults, their takes' average pitch score), each published version its own `pitchPercent` for comparing a part's versions, and the review queue `?singer=` to open one singer's takes with Hillel's notes. | Choosing whom to hire again, and each part's default, should take a glance. |
| **What a singer is paid is never shown to the singer in the app** (Hillel's rule, through his session): the hourly rate, minutes paid, what's owed, paid and payable, and the dates are only in the admin's Singers (`/api/admin/beta/singers*`, admin only, `paid` and `forget` in sudo). A test asks every singer-facing answer (beta.*, the account, the catalogue and templates.show) of a paid singer, and fails on any money key at any depth; another checks singers and editors get 403 from every admin singer route. No email to a singer mentions money. **Download my data does include a singer's own payment records** (`beta-singer.json`): their personal data, by the right of access, in their own export only. | Hillel's requirement; the export follows the law's right of access without putting money in the app. |
| **The admin's version badge** (Hillel, through the frontend): `/up` adds `builtAt` (written into the image at build time, `.do/Dockerfile`) beside `build`, and the app reads it at start and on focus (a different build: "Reload"); `account.json` gains `staff` (`admin`, `editor` or null), a display hint only, so ordinary pages never call an admin operation to decide whether to show the badge. | `/up` is public, cheap and already limited per network. |
| **A project's title may be blank** (from Hillel's decision of 2026-10-26, "a new project opens at once"; backend and frontend): `projects.save` takes 0 to 120 characters; the app shows "Untitled song" for a blank one, in lists, exports and shares. | The project opens at once, with no name asked. |
| **Fixes from M5's independent review** (backend): a chargeback finds its account through its charge (Stripe's dispute names no customer) and is applied once, when the account is known; production refuses every webhook while the signing secret is missing (and `/up` says degraded), and the tests sign theirs; each Stripe event is handled once (`stripe_events`), and a trial's card is recorded with its subscription, so a redelivery never cancels the person's own trial; a second trial on one account is cancelled; a card-free trial ends if no card arrives (`missing_payment_method: cancel`), and its card is checked when it's added; deleting the account cancels the subscription; after a downgrade which projects stay editable is settled then (the most recently edited), and filling a freed slot goes by the newest made, never by last edit, which a settings-only save moved; binning or restoring updates read-only for every device; deleting a part of a read-only project is refused; `billing.show`'s plan comes from the subscription (the portal may switch it); the return page waits for `checkout.session.completed` and Plus itself; failed payments are counted as security events for the card-testing alert. | Each could give Plus away, keep charging someone, or move a read-only line, shown step by step by the reviewer. |
| **Plus through Stripe Checkout and the portal, by Cashier** (backend and frontend, M5): two prices (monthly, yearly), each with EUR and GBP currency options; Stripe Tax; Plus kept while a payment is retried (`keepPastDueSubscriptionsActive`) and to the end of a cancelled period; our own webhook (`/webhooks/stripe`, Cashier's signature check) adds one trial per card, the renewal and retry dates, how Plus ended, disputes (trust level 0, Plus ends, listed in admin) and the two emails (trial ending, payment failed). Stripe is reached only through `BillingGateway` (the tests' fake never calls Stripe). The dependencies are the plans' own: `laravel/cashier` and `minishlink/web-push`. | docs/backend-plan.md D8, after Event Schedule; nothing we can't test without Stripe. |
| **The trial** (pricing open decision #3, as recommended): 7 days, once per account and once per card; yearly with a card up front for anyone, monthly without a card only from trust level 2. A card that had a trial ends the new one at once with no charge (`notice: card_had_trial`). Checkout needs a verified account, has a limit, and a switch (`pause_checkout`) for card testing. | Bots can't farm trials without singing, and a card can't buy two. |
| **After a downgrade, the person picks which projects stay editable** (pricing open decision #6, as recommended), the most recently edited by default; projects made from a Plus song are read-only until Plus. Read-only projects still save their practice settings; other changes answer `403 project_read_only`; becoming read-only changes a project's sync change number, not its version. M4's "the oldest stay editable" is replaced. | Nothing is deleted, nothing queued offline turns into a conflict. |
| **Plus songs come from `templates.show`** (M5): to Plus, to anyone (signed out too) for the song of the month, and for good to whoever started a project from it that month (`template_unlocks`, written when the project is made); a daily cap per account (or network) with an ETag, a 304 or a same-day repeat not counting; offline, usable until `plusOfflineUntil` (14 days after the last check, never past a cancelled plan's end). | docs/pricing-plan.md's "sample, not a lock wall" and "finish your song"; scraping is capped and flagged. |
| **The score history keeps everyone's scores, and shows them with Plus** (M5): a take's report arrives with the take (`takes.create`'s `scores`, under the take's id, replacing M4's `take_scores`), exercises by `scores.create`; `scores.list` is Plus. | Upgrading brings your history with you; nothing is sent twice. |
| **Practice reminders by web push** (M5): days, a local time on a quarter hour and the time zone; sent at most once a day by a job every 15 minutes (a missing local time on a clock change fires at the next one; a repeated one once); the payload is `{kind, url}`, never anyone's text; a device is changed or removed by its own token (the service worker has no session or CSRF); a gone subscription is forgotten. | docs/operations-plan.md; Safari revokes a push without a notification, so the worker always shows one. |
| **A Plus demo and Hillel's Plus by hand:** `users.plan = 'plus'` gives Plus without Stripe (a coach's free Plus, the demo's `plus@example.com`). | The plans' "coaches get Plus free while active", and a way to test Plus screens locally. |
| **Our name, never the framework's, wherever a default applies** (backend, from Hillel's session finding the beta's email from "Laravel"): `APP_NAME` and `MAIL_FROM_NAME` are `Stackapella` in `.do/app.yaml`; every config fallback (mail's from name, the session cookie, the cache and Redis prefixes, logging) is Stackapella; and in production `/up` reports itself `degraded`, and logs an error, while the app's name or mail sender is still a framework default. Setting the name renamed the session cookie, which signed the beta's sessions out once. | A fresh app can't miss it, and if it does, the health check says so. |
| **The app's own files always revalidate** (`Cache-Control: no-cache` with an ETag, in `.do/nginx.conf` and the server's own answers; backend, from the frontend's diagnosis of a stale admin on the beta): a module imported after load (the admin, Sing) was answered from the browser's cache for up to 5 minutes after a deploy, beside newer ones, and a hard reload doesn't reach those. The service worker revalidates too, and reloads once when a new one takes over. | Versions must never mix; an unchanged file costs a 304. |
| **Fixes from the frontend's sign-in review** (backend, M3 follow-up): the app pages' CSP allows the Google and Apple sign-in redirects (`form-action`) and Turnstile's script and frame (shield mode would otherwise lock everyone out); before launch, the emails' link pages (`/verify`, `/reset`, `/email/confirm`, `/email/revert`) pass the coming-soon gate and, like invite pages, send no referrer; an account that hasn't answered the age question may delete itself without a recent sign-in. | Each blocked a real path (a singer verifying in another browser, Google sign-in from the app, an under-13 answer after a provider sign-in). |
| **The app's email comes from `contact@stackapella.com`** (Hillel, through his session, 2026-10-26; `MAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` in production, and the default in `config/mail.php` and `.env.example`), not `hello@`. | One address people can reply to; it also receives the DMARC reports. |
| **How published versions work** (backend and frontend, from Hillel's decision of 2026-10-26): each version keeps its own id (`guide.recording.id`); the catalogue lists every published version of a part (`versions`, the default first, then the newest) beside the default (`guide`); a learner's choice is the app's, kept per part as a list of version ids (`settings.voices`, one item until the custom choir; `"synth"` reserved for the practice voice; an empty list means `guide`), and in `account.learning` for their own default; ids no longer published drop out. When the default is removed, the version Hillel made default most recently (`template_guides.defaulted_at`) takes over, else the newest, else the synth. Revoking a singer ends their role, not their published versions. The later custom choir fits as several version ids per part. | The data already held several versions per part; nothing needed rewriting, and no new operation was needed for the choice. |
| **How beta singers have accounts** (backend and frontend, from Hillel's decision of 2026-10-26): an invite is the singer record's hashed token, working once, for 14 days unless Hillel sets otherwise, revocable; it attaches the record (the role, the agreement, the parts and takes) to the account that accepts it. An open invite carried by `auth.register` or a Google or Apple sign-in gets that one sign-up through paused sign-ups, and the server accepts it itself once the address is proven (at verification or the set-password link; at once for Google or Apple), as the verification email often opens in another browser; `beta.invite.accept` covers someone already signed in. Only an account with a proven address can accept, so an unverified sign-up made with the singer's address can't claim them; one account is one singer. The beta session, its long-lived cookie and its device codes are gone. The credit is the account's name, read whenever it's shown (anonymous stays a choice), so a rename reaches learners on their next catalogue fetch: guide files carry no name. Revoking ends the role and keeps the account; deleting the account forgets the role (its guides come off the app and the CDN, payments stay). `account.json` gains `betaSinger`. | Hillel's call ("one way in for everyone, and singers manage their own credit"); the old links' properties are kept: no takeover through an invite, once, revocable. M2 wasn't deployed, so nothing needed migrating. |
| **Public media is fetched with CORS from any origin** (backend, M2 integration check, asked by the frontend): the app keeps guides in IndexedDB, so it fetches them with `mode: 'cors'`; the local stand-in (`tool/serve.php` serving `/media/` from `storage/app/media`) answers `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, and the Spaces media bucket's CORS rule must too (Hillel's item 20). | The files are public and cookie-free; `*` exposes nothing the CDN doesn't. |
| **Our X and Bluesky keys are read as `X_API_KEY`, `X_API_SECRET`, `X_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `X_ACCESS_SECRET`, `BLUESKY_HANDLE` and `BLUESKY_APP_PASSWORD`** (`config/services.php` `x` and `bluesky`; backend, M4 week, from Hillel's session, which set them in production and checked them without posting: X's `GET /2/users/me` read-write, Bluesky's `createSession`). iaw's names carry a per-site infix; ours don't, one account per network. The readiness check uses the same no-post calls, on demand or daily, as X bills reads. | The posting service (D7) is ported later; the names are fixed now so production's settings match. |
| **Fixes from M4's independent review** (backend): a sync pull reads everything in one snapshot, and every change number is taken in the same transaction as its row, so a pull can never pass a change; every change that checks a limit locks the account first (two devices can't both make the third project, or both fit the last megabyte); restoring a project counts its takes against storage (`403 storage_limit`); a project beyond the limit takes no new takes (`403 project_limit`); the purge checks each row again under a lock and deletes files only after the commit, so a restore a moment before wins; a take no part plays and nothing waits to replace goes to the bin after a day; an overtaken Undo keeps the first take waiting; upload URLs last 10 minutes, and a second PUT after completion is thrown away (the purge now runs hourly). | Each could lose a change, a recording or a limit, shown step by step by the reviewer. Spaces can't bind a presigned PUT to its size, so a lifecycle rule on `uploads/` is on Hillel's list (item 19). |
| **Cloud save keeps the device's shape** (backend and frontend, M4 contract): projects, parts and takes keep the device's UUIDs; typed fields where the server needs them (title, tempo, key, the template's id and version; a part's mix, nudge, trims and current take) and the app's own `settings` object stored as sent (256 KB a project, 16 KB a part). Every save and delete carries the `version` it was based on and a `changeId`, so a replay after a lost answer is never a conflict; a stale one gets `409 version_conflict` with the server's copy. | The device stays the working copy (C5); the server only needs what it checks, counts or shares. |
| **A take's offset belongs to the take** (frontend's review, M4): `offsetFrames` is immutable on the take, and the part keeps a `nudgeFrames` for its current take, so an Undo plays the old take where it was recorded. | With the offset on the part, restoring an older take played it at the newer one's place. |
| **sync.pull is ordered by a per-account change number** given inside each change's transaction (`users.sync_seq`, one atomic `LAST_INSERT_ID` statement), not by timestamps; deletions leave tombstones kept 90 days, and an older cursor answers `410 cursor_expired` (pull everything again). | A late commit is never skipped, and nothing claims to be complete when it can't be. |
| **A replaced take goes to the bin only once its replacement is ready**; parents come before children (`409 project_missing`, `part_missing`), and a deleted part stays deleted (`409 part_gone`, even for a new save of its id). | A device that records and never comes back mustn't leave a part silent; the outbox needs an order it can trust. |
| **Storage counts every take outside the bin, net of the part's current take for a new one** (backend, M4); free 1 GB and 3 projects, Plus 50 GB (`config/usage.php`, still proposed); `users.plan` until billing (M5). An unverified account's projects save but its takes wait. | Re-recording at the limit must work; the allowances are confirmed from real FLAC sizes later (pricing question 4). |
| **Takes are kept as 16-bit FLAC at their own rate** (backend, M4), peaks the maximum of each 512 frames; the upload WAV goes once the FLAC is safe; an upload never completed loses its file after a day, its row waiting for a retry. Deleting an account deletes every recording in the background. Remixes sharing takes (a reference count) waits for M6, when remixes exist. | Lossless and about half the WAV's size; nothing is kept that the person can't see. |
| **Trust level 2 counts 2 recorded parts in the cloud** (backend, M4); practice minutes join when they sync. | The only real singing the server can see yet. |
| **`chords` in the template schema moves to M5** (frontend, M4 week), written by the frontend with its chord work. | The roadmap put it in M3's contract; the frontend renders it and will define it when it builds it. |
| **"Paused" and "read-only" answer 423, not 503** (backend and frontend, M3 week; found on the live beta by Hillel's session): App Platform's edge, behind Cloudflare, turned an origin 503 into a bare 504 after retrying, so the app couldn't read `code: paused`. 503 is kept only for maintenance (`unavailable`) and `/up`. A paused sign-up is refused before validation. | Every refusal the app must word has to reach it; a 4xx passes the edge untouched. |
| **Fixes from M3's independent review** (backend): Apple's last step works only in the browser that started (its state cookie) and runs without a session, so Apple's cross-site POST can't replace the person's own; linking Google or Apple is bound to the account that asked and needs a recent sign-in; every undo link for an email change keeps working for its 7 days, the earliest wins, and an undo clears the password; signing up over an unverified account drops its unproven password and sends a set-password link instead of a verification; a taken address shows as pending like any other; wrong passwords are counted per address in the rate limiter, for addresses with no account too, and re-entering the password counts the same; asking for a reset costs the same time either way; a failed or lost export no longer blocks the next; only a person's latest handle is held; avatars up to 3000 px a side. | Each closed an account takeover, an enumeration or a dead end the reviewer showed step by step. |
| **Known gap, M3:** a Google or Apple sign-in linked by someone who briefly had the account (with a recent sign-in) survives an email undo and a password reset; the person removes it from Settings (it's listed there). Revisit with the trust and safety queue (a "methods added in the last week" list on the undo page). | Undoing links by time needs when each was added, which isn't stored yet. |
| **The admin's People screen comes with accounts, in M3** (backend; `admin.people.*`): find someone, see their sign-in methods, trust level and last sign-in, suspend and unsuspend, set the trust level by hand, and recover. **"Hillel adds a sign-in method" is done as recovery:** once support has checked it's the owner, the account gets the address they can read now (verified) and a link to choose a password; the old address is told, and every session ends. All behind sudo with a reason; staff accounts only from the console; the audit log keeps which address changed, never the addresses. | The operations plan puts account recovery support in M3, and accounts can't be run without suspend. Adding someone's Google or Apple by hand isn't possible without their sign-in, so a password is the method support can add. |
| **Handles are checked through look-alikes** (backend, M3): digits and symbols read as the letters they pass for, a "1" both as l and as i, accents dropped, "rn" as m and "vv" as w, each word between underscores too, against Stackapella's own names and an offensive-word list (`resources/data/offensive-words.txt`). Trust levels 0 and 1 are earned in M3; 2 counts practice from M4 and 3 a charge from M5. | The trust and safety plan's "never one of our names, also through look-alikes"; one reading of "1" let "stackape11a" through. |
| **`account.plan` is `"free"` until billing (M5)** (backend, M3). | The field is in the contract now so the app's Settings doesn't change shape later. |
| **Uploads stay a presigned PUT, and the server enforces the size** (backend, M2 review): Spaces' signer never binds `Content-Length`, so `complete` refuses and deletes a wrong-size or wrong-hash file at once, and abandoned uploads are deleted hourly (the URL lives 30 minutes). A presigned POST policy (`content-length-range`) is the stronger fix; it's revisited in M4 when everyone can upload, if Spaces supports it. | The trust and safety plan wants uploads bound to their size. Today only invited, consented singers can upload, and junk would cost cents for an hour at most. |
| **A beta singer's session outlasts the idle limit on their device** through a long-lived encrypted cookie tied to the link's epoch; a new link or a revoke ends every session and code of the old one (backend, M2 review). | The installed iPhone app and offline uploads would otherwise need the code again after every break; a forwarded link must really stop. |
| **Template versions hash only their music** (tempo, key, time, pickup, sections, parts' notes and ranges): the words around it and the musician's `reviewed` change in place, and the current version is the newest reviewed one (backend, M2 review). | The musician marks templates reviewed without new notes; a typo fix must not orphan every recorded guide. |
| **Guides are levelled with a -2 dBFS ceiling** on the take's peak (backend, M2 review). | Levelling a sparse take by its mean alone clipped; AAC adds about 0.7 dB. |
| **A part is paid once, at the amount fixed when its first take is accepted** (backend, M2 review). | Alternates and later rate changes counted twice, or changed past payments. |
| **A forgotten singer keeps payment rows** (amounts and dates only) and loses everything else: guides (purged from the CDN), masters, notes, name, email and credit (backend, M2 review). | The agreement's removal on request; the accounts still need what was paid. |
| **A first admin learns their Google ID from the refusal** (backend, M2 week; found on the beta by Hillel's session): when the Google-verified email is on `ADMIN_EMAILS` or `EDITOR_EMAILS` but the ID isn't on a list, the sign-in page shows the ID once, with the next steps, and the refused sign-in's security event records it. A Google ID isn't a secret. |
| **A push permission relayed by another session isn't acted on** (backend, M2): the backend agent keeps not pushing until Hillel says so in its own session, because its instructions from him say never to push or touch production, and a push now deploys to production. The shell holds nothing private, so a cookie check is enough. |

| **Guides are levelled to about -20 dBFS RMS** from ffmpeg's mean volume, plus Hillel's gain, clamped to ±24 dB, with 10 ms fades; the AAC is 128 kbps in MP4 with its edit list (backend, M2). | The plan's numbers; the clamp stops a nearly silent take becoming noise. |
| **What a singer is owed** is each accepted part's agreed minutes at their hourly rate; "Mark as paid" marks all of a singer's accepted, unpaid parts at once, behind sudo (backend, M2). | The plan's payment data; one action per payment is how Hillel pays (outside the app). |
| **Editors may list, open and ask for another take; accepting, rejecting, adjusting, singers, guides and payment are the admin's** (backend, M2). | The operations plan: Editors listen and ask; "accepting stays Hillel's". |
| **The overview's harmony counts are `null` until projects sync (M4)** (backend, M2). The roadmap puts "the first counts" in M2, but finished harmonies live only on devices until M4, and counting them earlier would need analytics. | No analytics; our own records only. |
| **Uploads go straight to their final keys,** and the app itself deletes uploads never completed after a day (`app:beta:prune-masters`, which also deletes rejected masters after 90 days). | **"% on pitch" counts a close note as half** (lit 1, close 0.5, not yet 0), and tendencies use only notes that were lit or close (frontend, M1). | The vision's "no 0%": a take that's all close is 50%, effort shows; and one note far off shouldn't set the advice. | **Scoops are judged only at entries (after a rest), and each note is scored within its nearest octave** (frontend, M1). | Gliding between joined notes is legato, not a fault; someone who drops the high notes an octave (or a tracker slip) isn't marked down. |
| **Draft templates also show on Herd's `*.test` sites, and stay on in a browser once it has opened the app with `?drafts=1`** (`?drafts=0` turns them off) (frontend, M1). | Hillel tests on `stackapella.test` and through tunnels whose address changes; drafts on the live site still need the explicit `?drafts=1`. |
| **A new part's default name ("Melody", "Part 7") and "Untitled song" are saved in the person's language,** like a document's default name; template part names stay keys and translate (frontend, M1). | They're the singer's own names once made, and can be renamed; storing keys for them would complicate renaming for little gain. |
| **The studio's timeline and the pan and timing sliders stay left to right in right-to-left languages** (frontend, M1). | Time runs left to right on a staff, and pan and nudge are physical directions. |

| **The server is deployed as one Docker image built from the repository's root** (backend, M1; `.do/Dockerfile`), not the PHP buildpack: nginx serves `/web`'s files directly and php-fpm runs Laravel, in every component (web, scheduler, the `long` worker, migrate). | **The limiters run before the sign-in check** (backend, M1), key by the user or a daily HMAC of the IP, and record a refusal in `security_events` at most once an hour per limiter and person (an hourly count is kept for the alert). | **`security_events` rows are kept a year,** with the raw IP cleared after 30 days (backend, M1). | The plan sets the 30 days for raw IPs but not the rows; a year covers repeat abuse. |
| **The commit hook lets either agent commit `docs/` and `README.md`** (each its own sections; plans only appended or given a status line), and `contract/log.md` needs no `CONTRACT_OK=1` (backend, M1). | Both agents append to the decisions and the log constantly. |
| **The N+1 guard is smaller than iaw's** (backend, M1): a request fails when it runs one query shape 4 or more times (the cache table aside), with deliberate repeats in `tests/query-baseline.php`. | **Locally, `php -S 127.0.0.1:8000 tool/serve.php`** in `server/` replaces `php artisan serve` (backend, M1). | A `git rebase` (every landing) recreates `public/`, which left `artisan serve` pointing at a deleted folder. |
| **The dependencies added are the ones the plans name** (backend, M1): `sqids/sqids`, `pragmarx/google2fa`, `bacon/bacon-qr-code`, `laravel/passkeys`, `laravel/socialite`, `league/flysystem-aws-s3-v3`, and `larastan/larastan` for development (named in the conventions). | Approved by "Working without Hillel". |
| **The mic opens when Record is pressed (the setup), and the first click waits 0.8 s after it opened, on the audio clock** (frontend, M0). | **The audio context is rebuilt at the mic's sample rate only on iPhone and iPad,** and in Firefox only when it refuses the mic (frontend, M0). | iOS switches its hardware rate when the mic opens; elsewhere a rebuild would only lower quality (a Bluetooth headset reports 16 or 24 kHz). |
| **Only the mic's `ended` event stops a take, not `mute`** (frontend, M0). | iOS mutes a track briefly while switching routes; stopping then would cut good takes. A call or a locked screen is caught by the context stopping. |
| **On an iPhone, "iPhone Microphone" is its own mic, not a wireless one;** the wireless note uses the roadmap's wording (frontend, M0). | The Continuity check is only for computers. |
| **An old tab blocking the database upgrade gets a message, and a newer tab's upgrade asks this one to reload** (frontend, M1). | Rather than a page that hangs, or saves that fail later. |
| **A beta take is sent as from the song's start:** one recorded from later, or nudged, is padded with silence or cut before it's kept to send (frontend, M2). | The submission has no offset field; Hillel's review and the published guide line every take up at beat 0. |
| **The room's noise is judged only where the singer isn't singing:** the take's rests (from 0.25 s after a note to 0.1 s before the next), and the sound check's first 2 seconds, now asked for in silence. | **The latency, timing kind and mic are stored with a take when it's recorded,** and sent from there (frontend, M2). | Read at sending time, a reload sent no mic and a latency of 0. |
| **M3's contract, agreed with three changes:** `elapsedMs` (measured on the device) for the sign-up bot check instead of a start time; the sign-in form's CSRF token as `_token` (the XSRF cookie's value), a stale one coming back as `?authError=expired`; and `account.email.revert` answering with the restored address (frontend and backend, M3). | A phone's clock ahead of the server's would have been taken for a bot and silently dropped; a form can't send the header; "Choose a new password" goes to the address in one tap. |
| **M4's contract, with nine changes from the frontend's review:** an immutable `offsetFrames` per take and `nudgeFrames` per part; a `changeId` on every save and delete, so a replay isn't a conflict; versions on deletes, and `part_gone`, `project_missing`, `part_missing`; a change-number cursor with `cursor_expired`; a replaced take binned only once its replacement is ready; storage counted net of the take being replaced; 8 to 192 kHz uploads (frontend and backend, M4). | Each one closes a way a take or a part could be lost, or a person asked about a conflict with their own change. |
| **`paused` and `read_only` answer 423, not 503** (backend, agreed by the frontend). | The hosting edge turns a 503 into an empty 504 after retrying; the app reads codes, so nothing else changes. |
| **The review's preview publishes as the server does:** the start trim in song time (after the nudge), the end trim from the take's end, and the level within 24 dB and never above -2 dBFS at the take's peak (frontend, M2). | So what Hillel hears is what learners get (server/app/Jobs/PublishGuide.php). |
| **A take sung to an older version's notes has no chart, phrase pinning or synth A/B in the review;** it's judged in the mix (frontend, M2). | The app only has the current notes; comparing against them would mislead. |
| **Fixes being tried are saved before moving to another take** (J, K, Back), and the review's single-letter keys can be turned off (frontend, M2). | Nothing set is lost by a key press; WCAG 2.1.4. |
| **A real voice's badge on the part says "Sung by" its credit** (frontend, M2). | The plan's "credit shows on the part"; the badge said only "Real voice". |
| **In beta mode, the message after a take offers Check and send, not the next part** (frontend, M2). | A beta singer records only the part Hillel asked for. |
| **Hillel assigns every beta part;** there are no open parts for singers to pick, and no range check to suggest them, in the beta (frontend and backend, M2). | The contract has none, and a beta of a few invited singers is easier to run by asking each for a part. Open parts can come later with the course's range finding. |
| **The beta welcome's 20-second sample shows only once the server sets a preview** (`previewUrl`); none is made yet (frontend, M2). | Making one is an admin tool for later; the welcome reads well without it. |
| **The sound check judges level, clipping and noise with nothing playing; bleed is checked on the take** (frontend, M2). | Bleed needs the other parts playing during the singer's rests, which only a real take has. |
| **Exported file names keep to the song and arranger; real voices' credits go in the file's notes (ICMT)** (frontend, M2). | Names with every singer's credit would be long and fragile across systems. |
| **A beta project's other parts use the real voices My parts sends** (the part's template version), not the catalogue's (frontend, M2). | On a Plus song the catalogue has none, so singers would learn against the synth; the plan wants earlier real voices to reach later singers. |
| **Takes waiting on the device are sent at launch, and a device that has signed into the beta links to "Your beta parts" on the projects page** (frontend, M2). | The installed app opens on the projects page; a take recorded offline shouldn't wait for My parts to be opened. |
| **A beta singer can send the take before their latest instead** ("Use my previous take instead" in the report), and "Hear it as learners will" plays it levelled as it will be published, ignoring the project's mutes and solos (frontend, M2). | The plan's "pick your best", and a preview at learners' level. |
| **Part minutes default to 30 in the invite form,** not worked out from the part's length (frontend, M2). | Learning time varies far more than the part's length; Hillel sets it per part. |
| **Turnstile's script loads from Cloudflare unpinned, with no integrity hash, and only in shield mode** (frontend, M3). | Cloudflare requires its script from its own address and changes it; it's the one exception to pinned libraries, never loaded otherwise. |

| **A new Google or Apple account is asked "13 or over?" once, wherever the person is; under 13, that account is deleted at once and the app carries on without one** (frontend, M3). | The provider's page can't ask; an account can't be kept for someone under 13, but singing needs no account. |
| **A change that needs a recent sign-in offers "Sign in again", which signs out and comes back to Settings** (frontend, M3). | Signing in again with a password needs to be signed out first; it's rare and plain. |

The part dialog lists every published voice (whose, and "Hillel's choice" marked) and the practice voice; several chosen voices are mixed into the part at an equal share, ready for the choir (frontend, Hillel's decision c7d0863 and its clarification). | One shape now and for the choir; the server keeps no choice. |
| **Recorded guides are kept on the device by version** (`templateId@version:partKey#id`) (frontend). | Several voices per part; a cached one plays offline when it's chosen. |
| **The admin's top bar has "Back to Stackapella" (the projects page, the admin staying signed in) beside Sign out, on every admin screen and on a phone** (frontend, Hillel's request). | A clear way out of the admin. |
| **The app's files are served `no-cache` with an ETag (nginx, by the backend), and the service worker revalidates every fetch of our own files, admin modules included; a page reloads once when a new version's worker takes over** (frontend and backend, after Hillel saw an old admin after a deploy). | With a max-age, the browser's memory cache answered modules imported after load (the admin, Sing) from before the deploy, even after a hard reload; a 304 costs little. tests/flows/deploy.py checks it. |
| **Slow practice (85%, 70%) slows the whole song** (guides, words, loops, the click and Get ready), takes step aside for their guides, and recording waits for full speed (frontend, M3). | A take is recorded in time with everything else at 100%; at 70% it would never fit. |

| **A raw translation key never shows:** a key the words in hand lack shows nothing while the words are fetched again past every cache (once per key), and the screen re-renders with them; the miss is logged as a warning, and the key check (tool/i18n_check.py) keeps every key in en.json (frontend, after Hillel saw keys on the sign-up page). | An old copy of the words beside new code is the only way a key goes missing in production; the app has no error-reporting channel yet. |

| **The 8 templates' chords are first drafts worked out from their own four-part arrangements** (beat by beat, passing notes smoothed, a pickup taking the first chord), for the musician to check with the notes (frontend, M3). | The arranger's harmony is already in the parts; the musician's review covers chords as well. |
| **Chords underneath: off by default, a Chords toggle in Sing with its level and Pad or Piano; the chords never go into a take, and exports leave them out unless "Include the chords" is ticked** (frontend, M3, feature 3). | As the plan says; a cappella by default. |
| **A template's preview plays all four parts from the start, and choosing the number of singers only mutes or unmutes parts (a few milliseconds' ramp), so it never restarts** (frontend, Hillel's request). | Changing it mid-song kept jumping back to the start. | **An admin (the account's `staff`, a display hint) sees a tiny version badge in the bottom corner of every screen: the build and when it was built, from /up, above the studio's and Sing's transport; when the server's build changes while the page runs it turns amber with "new version, reload"; a tap copies the build (or reloads, when stale). | **A lesson's stars come from its last sung step (an exercise or a recording); a lesson with nothing to score (learning, practising in a song) gives 3 stars when done; exercises are scored on pitch only, in any octave** (frontend, M3). | Units 4 to 7 are mostly songs: without this, practising them could never open the next unit. | **Trying an exercise isn't recorded (the live line reads the mic); only Sing it records and scores** (frontend, M3). | Nothing kept that isn't used. |

| **The share video's words and waveforms are drawn with GD, and ffmpeg only moves and encodes them** (backend, M6; `ShareVideoRenderer`), instead of ASS subtitles with `\k` timing (the plan's how): each syllable's moment is one picture of its line, shown until the next, from `Karaoke` (the template's syllables at the project's tempo, tested to the beat). | macOS's ffmpeg has no libass or drawtext, so ASS couldn't be tested where the agents work; GD gives the card's font and look in one renderer; ffmpeg needs only overlay and H.264. | **The device sends each part's outline (a byte per 50 ms, after its volume), where beat 0 falls in the mix, and which published voices are in it** (backend, M6 contract): the server draws the video's and card's waveforms from them, times the words from its own copy of the template, and credits the voices from its own records. Credits and words never come from the request. | The server never has the stems, and never remixes; but text on our pages and videos must be ours. |
| **Share videos are kept under `exports/videos/` in `private`, not `tmp/`** (backend, M6). | The bucket's lifecycle rule deletes `tmp/` after 1 day and `exports/` after 7: the plan's 7 days. |
| **A share counts when a harmony becomes unlisted** (made unlisted, or changed to it), by trust level (`shares`: 3, 20 and 50 a day), counted by the action rather than the route; making a private one counts toward nothing, and asking for the same video again doesn't count as a video. Sharing paused answers `423`; videos paused wait `queued`, as the switch's description says. | The limit is on links let loose, not on keeping a mix. |

| **A title is refused only for a link; a title on the word lists (slurs, scam and spam phrases, a phone number or email, a long run of one character) is shared as "Untitled song" until Hillel looks** (backend, M6; `TextFilter`). | **The choir plan, for launch (pricing plan #11; for Hillel to confirm, Needs Hillel):** groups come with the director's Plus at no extra price; up to 60 members a group and 5 groups a director; members join free and learn the group's songs, Plus songs included, while they're in it (their projects from them stay editable); if the director's Plus ends the group stays but takes no new songs, members or assignments. The director sees only names and each assignment's status (none, started, recorded), never recordings or contact details. **Until Hillel decides how members under 18 take part, groups are 18 and over** (`usage.group_min_age`, asked once on making or joining one; `403 adult_required`), as the beta is. | Choir directors are the likeliest to pay; a seat price can come later without changing the shape. | **Cancellations follow the coach's policy** (`cancellationHours`, 24 by default; backend, M9): cancelled by the coach (in ES or here) is a full refund; by the singer that many hours ahead or more, a full refund; later than that, no refund and the coach's share moves. | **A take keeps at most 16384 peaks** (backend with the frontend's agreement, the review of 2026-10-27, H2): `framesPerPeak` is 512, doubled for a long take until it fits; takes already stored are thinned by a migration. | **A project may name someone's template only if its owner may open it then** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, M1): `projects.save` refuses (`422` on `template.id`) a user template that isn't theirs, unlisted or shared with a group they're in. A project already made from it keeps it (and its singers the `project` grant), as a song of the month's projects stay usable after the month. | A private template's id alone gave it, and its creator's recorded voices, to anyone who named it in a project. |
| **Friends on a harmony** (backend with the frontend's agreement, the review of 2026-10-27, M2): a harmony records who else sang the takes in its mix (`singer_ids`). They're credited after the owner. Featuring and the gallery wait for each of them (`409 singers_must_agree` until N1 asks them); an unlisted link doesn't, as the plan asks consent only before a harmony is public. It comes down when a friend leaves taking their parts, or deletes their account. Existing harmonies with a friend lost any yes to featuring or the gallery. | Their voice is theirs: the owner alone shouldn't put it on our social accounts or in the gallery, nor keep it playing after they asked for it to go. |
| **A lesson is done only after the end it was bought for** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, M3): lessons keep `agreed_ends_at`, and count as done (the coach paid) only once it and the current end have both passed. A coach's move with less notice than their policy lets the singer cancel in full until the new start. | A coach (or a self-hosted ES's signed webhook) could move a lesson into the past and mark it done to be paid at once. A lesson moved earlier is still paid, by the hourly sweep, after its original end. |
| **Sign in with Apple sends a nonce and checks it** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, M4): a random nonce is kept with the sign-in's state (cached 10 minutes), sent to Apple, and checked against the id_token's `nonce` claim on the callback. | The stateless callback without one was refused by the library every time, so Apple sign-in could never have worked once configured; the nonce also binds the id_token to this sign-in. The tests fake Apple, so a real sign-in is still to be tried (Needs Hillel item 17). |
| **What's processed is a copy no upload link points at** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, M5 and M6): every upload's `complete` (takes, harmonies, review media, template recordings, beta masters) copies the file on the bucket to a fresh key, deletes the upload key, checks the copy's size and SHA-256, and claims it; one complete per upload at a time (a cache lock), and another at that moment answers as it is. Beta masters are copied within `beta/masters/`, the rest to `uploads/received/`. | S3 doesn't sign a presigned PUT's Content-Length, so the file at the upload key could be swapped, or padded to gigabytes, after `complete` checked it, and parallel completes each hashed the whole file in a web process. |
| **Download my data holds everything we keep about the person** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, M7 and M8): beside the readable files, `records/` has a file per kind (pitch history, support, notices and appeals, reports sent, coaching on both sides, lessons, Plus and payments, devices and passkeys by name, Event Schedule, choir parts, share videos, invites; a beta singer's notes, device, mic and feedback; the age answers). Each lists only the person's own fields: never another person's id, a secret, a device's push address or a file's key (recordings are links). Lists are streamed to temporary files, and take outlines never read. Left out on purpose: the security log beyond sign-ins (abuse records), the admin's notes about them, other people's data in shared projects. | The privacy policy promises everything we hold, and a heavy account's export ran out of memory and failed for good. |
| **A deleted buyer leaves our money records** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, M9): coaching payments keep their amounts and dates, and their line says "for A deleted account"; our book rows for a coach's sale (its fee and refunds) and a card Plus payment lose the name and address, so one not yet sent goes without them, and a coach's commission lines lose the singer's name. A coach's commission invoices and Plus by invoice keep the business's name. What Invoice Ninja already holds is the coach's or Stackapella's books, kept as tax law asks. | Decision 249 and the privacy draft promise money records "without names"; the text and JSON still had them. Invoices for a person (B2C) don't need the buyer's name; a business's do. |
| **One share video renders at a time, and the cache is pruned daily** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, M10 and M11): `sharing.videos.concurrent` is 1, below the two `long` workers, so takes and mixes never wait behind videos; `app:cache:prune` (03:50) deletes expired rows of the database cache and its locks in batches. | Two renders of up to 290 seconds held both long workers; limiter keys per IP are rarely read again, so Laravel never deleted them and the table grew without end. |
| **Review lows, outbound and money** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, lows 1 to 7): an Event Schedule delivery acts on the lesson its booking id names, and on a lesson by our engagement's id only before it has a booking id; a delivery that fails every try is forgotten so ES's redelivery is handled; reminders go only to devices on the push services' list (others are dropped); a self-hosted Event Schedule's or Invoice Ninja's answer stops at 2 MB, and errors quote at most 300 characters of it; a Connect `account.updated` reads the account from Stripe rather than trusting the event; CORS is closed (`config/cors.php`); an exercise pack keeps its description, price and currency as approved, and sells and shows at those. | Each closed a way for a booking, a host, a stale event, another site or an unreviewed edit to act where it shouldn't. |
| **Review lows, coaching and deletion** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, lows 8 to 16): a choir group's Plus songs are free to its singers only while its director has Plus (one month of Plus no longer gives up to 300 people the library for good); a coach deleting their account is paid for a delivered review not yet released, or the singer refunded if it can't move; bought exercise packs outlive the coach (unbought ones go) and ratings outlive the singer, without names; the editor sees no `paidAt`; a take or guide finishing as its owner is deleted leaves no file and publishes nothing (the singer is forgotten first, under the lock a publish takes); deletion runs before signing out, so a failure answers while still signed in; files are deleted 100 keys a job; support messages go two years after their last word, or with the account. | Each was money, a voice or a person's words left in the wrong place, or a promise the code didn't keep. |
| **The server asks for the age answer too, and sent posts come down** (backend with the frontend's agreement, the review of 2026-10-27, lows 17 and 18): uploading, sharing, joining a project or choir and buying coaching answer `403 age_unconfirmed` until "I'm 13 or over" is answered (decision 210 kept the gate in the app; this backs it up); a post already on X or Bluesky about a harmony is deleted there when its singer withdraws their yes, or deletes it or their account. | An API call could skip the app's question; a singer's no should reach what we already posted. |
| **Review lows, robustness** (backend, the review of 2026-10-27, lows 19 to 22): ffmpeg's encodes stop at 200 seconds, under their jobs' 300, and each queue worker sweeps its /tmp of our files over an hour old (hourly, from the worker loop), since a killed job never reaches its `finally`; the scheduler runs the heartbeat first and every slow task in the background; the bin purge deletes upload files without a HEAD request each; `takes.binned_at`, `takes.replaces_take_id` and `projects.binned_at` are indexed; the admin's singers list loads no outlines. Left as they are, fine at this size and noted for later: the overview's counts over whole tables, and the file backup's full listings. Migrations keep dates after the latest one (now 2026-11-04), never today's, so they always sort after the tables they touch. A received upload copy lives under `uploads/`, which the bucket expires after a day: a job retried later than that fails, and the app sends the take again (as for any failed take). | Each kept a slow or killed task from stalling or filling the small instances. |
| **The mudeo email's link and list, as built** (backend with the frontend's agreement, from Hillel's "telling mudeo's users"): the link is `/early/<code>`, not `/join/<code>`, which is already the project invite page. It lets an account through paused sign-ups (password and Google or Apple), marks it with the link, holds at its cap (500 to start) and five sign-ups an IP a day, and can be paused or replaced (the old code answers `closed`; an unknown code says the same, so codes can't be probed). The import skips invalid, duplicate, test-domain and suppressed addresses, and, until Hillel answers open question 3, addresses that already have a Stackapella account. Bounces join the suppression list with opt-outs. After 90 days only the opt-outs' addresses stay; their names and mudeo details go. | The plan, built; the path changed so two kinds of link never share a page. |
| **The mudeo email, sent through the app, as built** (backend with the frontend's agreement, from Hillel's change to sending through the app from mudeo.stackapella.com): Send the next batch reserves up to 50 and a queued job (on `long`) sends one email each from "Hillel from Stackapella" <hillel@mudeo.stackapella.com> (`MAIL_MUDEO_FROM`), Reply-To contact@stackapella.com, with the words in `lang/en/mudeo.php`. A daily cap (200, editable) replaces Gmail's limits. The footer and `List-Unsubscribe` headers carry a signed `/unsubscribe/<id>`: GET shows one button, POST suppresses (RFC 8058). A permanent bounce Cloudflare reports marks the contact bounced. Sending and the test refuse until `MAIL_MUDEO_READY` (set once a test passes SPF, DKIM and DMARC) and `MAIL_POSTAL_ADDRESS` are set. Someone it couldn't reach waits for a later batch; an app-sent batch can't be undone. The copy fallback stays. | Hillel's call; the ready switch makes "not set up" a clear refusal rather than a failed batch. |
| **Before launch, the app opens only with a beta pass** (backend, from 9c's finding and Hillel's ask for a preview link): a signed, HttpOnly cookie (`sa_pass`, 400 days) given by an open beta invite's page, the open early-access link's page, Hillel's preview link (`/preview/<code>`, managed like the early link in admin, never opening sign-ups), or any API call while signed in. The gate lets through the admin, `/signin`, the emails' token pages, and invite pages only while their token opens something; a session cookie, `/beta` or a made-up token no longer do. The coming-soon page's sign-in link goes to `/signin`. | Opening `/beta` set a session cookie that opened `/` for anyone. A page loaded through `/signin` can still move around inside the app, which the gate (full page loads only) can't stop; the app holds nothing private. |
| **The landing, pricing and help pages, as built** (backend, from Hillel's "who builds the public pages"): the landing is `/` for a first-time visitor, and the app for anyone who has used it (the app's `sa_app` marker, a session, a remember cookie) or asks for it (`?app`, the installed app's start), never cached as the landing (`Vary: Cookie`). It leads to lesson 1 (`/learn/1-comfortable`), draws the four voices as the app's home does, lists songs, shows the beta singers' stories from `config/pages.php` (none until they say yes), and the plans. `/pricing` follows `docs/features.md`, in pounds in the UK, euros in the euro area and dollars elsewhere (Cloudflare's country, then the browser's language, or `?currency=`), from `config/billing.php`; with Stripe's prices unset it says Plus isn't open yet. `/help` and `/help/mic`, `/help/headphones`, `/help/timing` and `/help/iphone` use the app's own advice. All are in the sitemap, with words in `lang/en/pages.php` and views in `views/pages/` on the frontend's `public.layout`. | The root URL must say what Stackapella is to search and to launch-week visitors; returning singers must never see it. |
| **The gallery (N1), as built** (backend with the frontend's agreement; behind the `gallery` flag): a harmony is in the gallery when unlisted, `inGallery`, live, not held, and its owner's profile is public; `inGallery` stays the opt-in before it opens. Anything public about a person (a public profile, a harmony in the gallery, comments, follows) needs 18 or over (`adult_confirmed_at`), so under-18s keep share links only. Putting one in once open needs trust level 2, a public profile, "I have the right to share this song" (which also licenses its parts as guide voices, Hillel's call), and each friend's yes (asked in the app, withdrawable, which takes it out); a level-2 singer's first two wait for review. Likes need a verified account, comments level 2 (limits from the trust and safety plan), one the word lists match is held; two different people's reports hold a comment. Blocks work both ways (profiles, harmonies, comments, follows). The bell (likes grouped per harmony a day, comments, follows, consent, featured) is in the app only and answers while closed. A hidden comment gives its author a `comment_hidden` notice with an appeal. The account shows `gallery: { canPost, canComment, postsHeld }`, never a trust level. Download my data and deleting an account cover it all. | D6 and the trust and safety plan, with the Children's Code's private-by-default for under-18s. |
| **Guide voices from public songs, as built** (backend with the frontend's agreement, from Hillel's "public songs' voices can become guides"): public means featured (`mayFeature`) or in the gallery, live, with every friend's yes; an unlisted link alone never is. Hillel publishes a part (`admin.gallery.guides.create`, sudo) from one of our templates; its take is levelled as a beta guide is and becomes a version of that template part's voice, credited by the singer's profile name. The terms (section 4a), the privacy draft and `/help/guides` say the same. | So the app, the terms and the help say one thing, and the licence ends exactly when the song stops being public. |

| **Leagues (N2), as built** (backend with the frontend's agreement; behind the `leagues` flag): XP is read from the course progress the app already syncs (`progress.xp.days`), each day capped at 300 as the app caps it, with a day over the cap (or in the future) clamped and flagged, so no new XP endpoint. Joining needs 18 or over, a handle and a verified account; the Friends tab (you and the people you follow who joined) is everyone's. Shown: handle, name, avatar, level, week's XP; blocks hide both ways. Also: comments and follows now obey the `pause_comments` and `pause_follows` switches (`423 paused`), and the six network features each have their flag. | The vocal coaching plan's section 8, keeping its gentleness and its honest-XP rules without asking the app for more. |

A batch stops after 4 minutes, and whoever it didn't reach waits for the next. An address suppressed elsewhere after the batch was picked comes off the list without counting as that batch's bounce. The 90-day clean-up keeps, for each person it mailed, only a keyed hash of the address: a re-import skips them and their old unsubscribe link still works. | A cold list mailed twice brings complaints and costs the sending domain its reputation. |
| **The preview link counts on a button, not on opening** (backend, same review): `/preview/<code>` shows one "Open the preview" button whose POST gives the beta pass and counts it. Deleting an account removes the guide voices credited to it, file and CDN copy too. A comment is held by two people under the page rule (never new accounts, one signed in and verified); Hillel's approve or hide answers its open reports. Five a day. A creator's first two wait for review unless they're level 3; reports hold one by the shared-page rule (3 networks, one signed in and verified). Hillel approves, hides (a `template_hidden` notice, appealable; projects keep their copy) or shows again; the Featured shelf is his, in sudo. Public templates are free and unlimited, never counted toward the free limit nor read-only after a downgrade. Held opens as if unlisted, hidden for its owner alone. Search covers the title, description and credits; filters by difficulty, voicing count, genre, key, tempo band and part range; sorted by uses (recounted nightly, people other than the creator), newest, or a rating that starts from 3.5 worth 3 ratings. Remixing is a first save with `parentId` (a public template or an official song the person may start). The terms (section 3) and the copyright policy now name the library. | The plan's Phase 3, with its safeguards first; ratings only from singers keep them honest, and a prior keeps one 5-star rating from topping the list. |
| **Ideas, as built** (backend with the frontend's agreement, from Hillel's step 3 of docs/your-tune-plan.md; behind the `ideas` flag): an idea is the writer's own template shared with every part of one voicing open, melody first; one per template, and its tune opens for anyone while the idea is listed (`grant: "idea"`). The writer and every singer must be 18 or over with a verified email, each confirming their rights; a public profile isn't needed (credited by name, with a handle only when the profile is public). Sorted by suggestion (Hillel's featured first, then the singer's voice type, then styles matching the song's genres, then rating), rating, price or soonest lesson; pages are numbered (the cursor is the page). A take is sent with its `pitchPercent`, `timingPercent` (null when too few moments to time), `verdict` and `tries`; the server refuses one whose pitch is missing or under the bar, or whose timing is under it, with `422 below_bar` and the bar, since the app can't be the only gate. | **Open parts (N5), as built** (backend with the frontend's agreement; behind the `open_parts` flag, the shelf behind the gallery's too): the owner of a public harmony (featured or in the gallery, every friend's yes, 18 or over) on one of our songs or a public template opens some of its parts, leaving one or more sung; the takes in its shared mix for the other parts are copied as **stems** (with their placement and outline), kept with the harmony and gone when the parts close or it's deleted. A filler (18 or over, verified, not blocked) hears the stems and sings the open part in their own project from the same song at the same tempo, and shares their own mix with `parentId` and `fillPart`; the server sees only their take, and lets the mix run as long as the stems. - 2026-10-27, frontend agent: Make it sound good (feature 1) is worked out entirely in code (`js/polish.js`), not with the browser's compressor and convolver: Chrome's compressor looks ahead a few milliseconds (it would move the singing), and its offline render differed slightly run to run, which broke "the render is identical on a second run". So: loudness by BS.1770-4 (K-weighting designed as the standard's reference does, so at 48 kHz it is the published coefficients exactly; 400 ms blocks, absolute and relative gates); each voice brought to -24 LUFS (the melody 1.5 dB forward, the chords 8 dB back, at most 18 dB either way), then a delay-free soft-knee compressor (-26 dB, 2.5:1); equal-power panning with a small spread by voice (the lowest in the middle); a seeded stereo room (decaying, darkening noise, unit energy) by FFT overlap-add under the mono mix (Small room 0.7 s at 16%, Hall 2.1 s at 24%, Chapel 3.4 s at 30%); then -14 LUFS and a look-ahead limiter at -1 dBFS (5 ms down, 80 ms back up), twice so limiting never leaves it quiet. Takes recorded at another rate are resampled by the browser first.

- 2026-10-27, frontend agent: the creator's "Suggest harmony" is reworked after a choral arranger's review (a music-theory subagent ran it on the library's songs and on folk, pop, Dorian, Mixolydian and minor tunes).
  - Chords are planned for the whole song at once (a Viterbi search over the slots). Progressions are scored (ii and IV to V, V to I, vi to ii or IV, no V back to IV, a seventh resolving). The song ends on I with V, IV or modal bVII before it.
  - A melody note a semitone from a chord now costs double.
  - Modal tunes get their own chords: Mixolydian gets bVII and v, Dorian IV major and bVII, natural minor v and bVII.
  - The planner steers away from changes where the tune and a root bass would make parallel octaves or fifths.
  - Each chord is voiced as a whole (alto, tenor and bass together), and the voicings are chosen across the song for the least cost:
    - motion and leaps (tritones heavily);
    - parallel fifths and octaves between any two voices, and hidden ones into the outer voices;
    - the third present, and kept in the alto or lower voices so the three-singer version keeps it;
    - no doubled leading tone;
    - the leading tone and seventh resolving;
    - spacing within an octave above the tenor;
    - each part near the middle of its range;
    - the bass well under the tune, on the root (Warm allows first inversions), and in root position at the end.
  - Between chord changes the inner voices hold and sing the words; they move only to stay under the tune or off a second against a strong or long note.
  - A tune written low becomes the soprano an octave up.
  - Beats compare to 1e-3, because the creator rounds them to 4 decimals and 6/8 cadences were being missed.
  - Measured with the reviewer's checker over 30 arrangements: 694 problems before, 134 after. Unisons dropped from 249 to 0 and parallels from 104 to about 10. Most of what's left is held inner notes, which choirs sing happily.
  - Five rule-check unit tests guard it: parallels at changes, no unisons or crossing, spacing and bass height, thirds and the root-position ending, smooth inner voices, Dorian chords.

  - The cookie's domain comes from the server: `<meta name="theme-cookie-domain">` with `hosts.pass_domain`, written by public.layout and (asked of ea) the app shell. Without it the cookie is host-only; nothing is guessed from the hostname.
  - Signed in, the account's saved theme wins. It's adopted through choose(), so the cookie and the public site follow too. A reply to this device's own save never overrides a newer choice.
  - The app's picker and the landing's light/dark switch both go through choose().
  - A new `theme_hosts` flow crosses two hosts of one domain (headless Chrome maps *.stackapella.test to this machine): chosen on one, the other opens in it, and back again.
- 2026-10-27, frontend agent: the song builder's music theory is web/js/theory.js: data plus pure functions, importing nothing, so the mobile app ports it as is.
  - 22 progressions in seven families (Foundations, Pop, Warm and soulful, Minor, Classic, Folk and modal, Blues), each with a friendly name, a one-line feel and tags. Two minor ones that start away from home (rising bVI–bVII–i, and iv–bVII–bIII–bVI) were added so a minor verse always has three choruses.
  - The blues: five forms (12-bar, quick change, 8-bar, minor, 16-bar), six turnarounds and four feels (shuffle, straight, slow and swung, gospel), with dominant sevenths on I, IV and V. Slow 12/8 is 4/4 with triplet swing, so no new time signature is needed.
  - suggestChorus gives three, ranked lift, contrast and classic. Each starts on a different chord from the verse, lands on I (V before it) and is sung about a third higher.
  - suggestBridge gives two or three that start where neither the verse nor the chorus did, and end on V.
  - suggestKey places the tonic so a typical melody (a fifth below to a sixth above for a verse, more for a chorus) sits inside the singer's comfortable range with a semitone to spare, the simpler key winning a near-tie, with Higher and Lower.
  - 390 test vectors in web/tests/vectors/theory.json, written by web/tool/vectors.py, with a unit test that they still match the module.

- 2026-10-27, frontend agent: theory.js after a songwriter's review.
  - Chords are spelled by letter for their key (C's bVII is Bb, A's iii is C#m). There's a diminished seventh for gospel's #iv°7.
  - A section's ending never changes its length: one ending on I, IV, V or bVII leads on as it is, and anything else ends on V. lastChorus() writes a full close (IV V | I, the triumphant bVI bVII | I, or iv V | i in minor) for the last chorus.
  - Choruses stay in the verse's mode: no more parallel-minor choruses for major verses. A minor verse's contrast is its relative major in its own numerals (bIII bVII bVI bVII). The classic pairing may share the verse's first chord.
  - New progressions: the royal road (IV V iii vi), the climb (IV V I I), the party loop (I IV V IV), barbershop (I VI7 II7 V7), minor sway (i bVII bVI bVII) and minor sunlit (i bIII bVII IV). Minor home is i iv V i.
  - The blues:
    - the jazz turnaround is I bIII7 | bVI7 bII7;
    - the minor blues is the "Thrill Is Gone" shape (bVI7 V7 in bars 9 and 10, and its own turnaround whatever's chosen);
    - Straight uses plain triads;
    - Gospel passes IV7 #iv°7 in bar 6 and IV iv in bar 10.
  - Bridges are eight bars, with V held for the last two. A blues gets the rhythm-and-blues bridge. There's a pre-chorus (ii IV V V or IV IV V V; bVI bVII in minor).
  - The key chooser:
    - narrower melody spans (verse a fourth below to a fifth above; chorus and bridge a minor third below to a sixth above);
    - one key for the song's sections together;
    - untrained voice ranges;
    - headroom of one semitone, or two at the top for a range check's extremes;
    - a margin of up to two semitones counts, and then a guitar-friendly key wins (C G D A E F Bb, Am Em Dm).

- 2026-10-27, frontend agent: The beta take bar's numbers (from Hillel's "beta singers' scores"): a guide is scored stricter than practice, lit within 20 cents ("precise") and timed within 70 ms. `timingPercent` is the share of timed moments (each entry after a rest, and each change to a note a semitone or more away) within 70 ms of the singer's own usual offset. With the latency measured, that offset is held to 100 ms of the beat, since an evenly late take lines up with a shift; with it estimated, any offset counts. Fewer than 3 moments means no timing score, and the take is judged on pitch. The defaults are 90 pitch and 80 timing (agreed with ea, 51bef2e). Calibrated on synthesized takes (`tests/quality.test.js`): a good singer (within 10 cents, 35 ms) is Ready, a careful amateur (28 cents, 70 ms) is Close, a poor one (70 cents, 180 ms) is Not ready yet.

- 2026-10-27, frontend agent: Hillel's iPhone mic failure (Chrome on iOS 26, Find your note) showed only "couldn't start": the exercise screens printed that one line whatever the error was, so the real cause wasn't seen. The likeliest is WebKit bug 319706, where iOS 26 loses its audio capture device ("NotAllowedError: No AVAudioSessionCaptureDevice device") until the browser is quit. Fixed in WebKit in summer 2026, but not in every 26.x. What changed:
  - The mic help names this case and says to quit the browser, or restart the phone.
  - Opening the mic now tries the chosen mic, the default, plain `audio: true`, and on iOS once with the audio session left to the browser.

  - The page has a proper media session: Stackapella by name, a pause that stops as a call would, "playing" only while playing. That is why his Now Playing card appeared.

- 2026-10-27, frontend agent: Hillel's 422 loop on templates.save had one cause in the transcriber, with two effects.
  - The cause: a short scoop was folded into the next note by moving the note's start, but not its pitch readings. A long scoop into a very short note left the note's middle empty, so its pitch was NaN.
  - Fixed: the readings come along, and a note with no pitch to give is dropped, never NaN.
  - Templates and drafts are repaired before they're kept or sent: such notes go, ranges refit, and chords from the tune's end on go. A Start from chords song hummed for less than all its loops had chords past the end, also refused.
  - A 422 is shown with every message the account gave, on the template's page and as "saved on this device only", and isn't resent until the template changes.
  - The note roll ignores a non-finite pitch, which had made its SVG coordinates NaN.
  - Several new badges at once are one toast, so they no longer stack over the stars.
