- 2026-10-27, landing agent: The chords step (86.png). In Shape, a chord's colour and height come from theory.js chordRole (97's): Home is the tonic and its relative (I; vi and iii, or III in minor), Pull back home is the dominant family (V, vii), and Lift is everything else. Height is how far from home it feels, so V stands tallest (where the mockup drew vi tallest; its heights were illustrative). The blues stays one card; changing its shape, turnaround or feel chooses it, so it needs no Choose button either. The footer folds a chord held for bars on end ("C7 ×4") so a 12-bar fits. The header keeps the frame's "Make your own · Step 1 of 5" rather than the mockup's "New song", so every step reads the same. The step names wrap to two lines on a phone instead of cutting off.

## 2026-10-26 (email through Cloudflare)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Transactional email goes through Cloudflare Email Service,** not Postmark: raw MIME through its REST API, with a small custom Laravel transport (no new dependency), a `failover` mailer ready, suppressions synced into our records every 15 minutes, and the quota and rates watched. | Hillel's call; it sits with Cloudflare's DNS and edge, which we use anyway. |
| **Noted:** Email Sending is in public beta (since April 2026), with a daily quota that grows, and it needs the Workers Paid plan. | The failover mailer and the quota alert cover the risk. |

## 2026-10-26 (everything that doesn't need a network, at launch)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The launch includes everything that doesn't need a network:** the template creator (shared by link), collaboration, choir groups, coaching without its directory (coaches sell through their own profile link), Event Schedule, Invoice Ninja, the harmony gym, posts of new songs. | Hillel's call: if a feature works for one person, or for people who already know each other, ship it. |
| **What waits is what needs strangers to find each other:** the gallery, the template library's search, the coach directory, leagues, the open parts shelf, the virtual choir, song requests (N1 to N7). Opt-ins are collected from launch. | Those features are empty, or worse, without a crowd. |
| **An admin "network features" panel** tracks each one against a threshold (proposed: e.g. 300 gallery opt-ins from 100 singers; 15 approved coaches with ratings and 500 weekly singers), recounted nightly, with the status Collecting, Start building, Ready to open, or Open. Opening one is a feature flag. | Hillel's call: know when to build and show each feature, from our own records. |
| **Launch readiness (L) can follow any milestone from M6** if Hillel decides to launch with less. | The bigger launch is a real cost; the core still comes first. |

## 2026-10-26 (languages)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **English at launch, but every language a drop-in:** every user-facing word through `t()` (app) or `__()` (server), the API sends codes not sentences, `Intl` for dates, numbers and prices, content data with text by language, songs keeping their own language, logical CSS for right-to-left, fonts that fall back by script, and `users.locale`. From M1, with a missing-key test and a pseudo-language. | Hillel's call: adding a language should only mean adding translation files, never rewriting screens. |
| **Our own small `i18n.js`** (`Intl.PluralRules` for plurals), not a library. | No npm, no new dependency; the need is small. |

## 2026-10-26 (a product review)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Intervals come into the free course** (Find the third, Hold your note, in units 5 and 6); relative-tuning feedback follows later with the gym. | Matching a guide isn't hearing harmony; the blend is the real skill. |
| **Slow practice (85%, 70%) comes in M3,** with the synth; real voices stay at 100%. | One of the most asked-for learning tools, and the synth makes it free. |
| **Re-sing a phrase in M5:** the phrase is replaced sample-exactly with short crossfades, as a new take. Free-form punch-in stays later. | One hard phrase shouldn't force a whole retake. |
| **Practice tracks** (everything but my part, my part louder, with a click) **and sharing just my part,** free at launch. | Serves choir members and section leaders without choir groups. |
| **What's next after a finished song:** range, difficulty, the song of the month, another part; from M7, finish counts and "also learned" from our own records. | The second song is the measure that matters. |
| **Note labels** (letters, solfège, numbers) **and a keyboard on the lane; a Storage page; a kind note when the real voice can't follow the key.** | Small changes that remove friction for learners. |
| **Not adopted as launch work:** relative-tuning scores in the report (later, with the gym). **Noted:** two new songs a month is ambitious; the synth-first rule (real voices as singers are ready) keeps it realistic. | Scope. |

## 2026-10-26 (a third review: the agents' habits)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Both role files open with "Never break these"** (ten invariants) **and "Every task starts the same way":** rebase, read the log's Now block and last 10 entries, newer decisions win, and proposed items are confirmed with Hillel before they're built. | The risk is less missing rules than agents not re-reading the right one under pressure. |
| **`contract/log.md` gets a "Now" block:** the milestone, the IndexedDB version (and claims), the operations being built, the stubs landed, and the questions waiting on Hillel. | One place to see where things stand after a restart or a missed message. |
| **IndexedDB versions are claimed in the log before they're used;** **public stub views are the first frontend commit** of a milestone, and the backend checks they've landed before its page tests. | Coordination steps that are easy to forget. |
| **M1 adds a short testing guide** (a unit test, a flow script, a mock scenario, each with an example). | `cdp.py` flow scripts are less familiar than a usual test runner. |
| **The retrospective asks the same four questions after each milestone,** starting with what the agents missed or invented. | So the docs improve where they actually failed. |
| **Not adopted:** a separate one-page quick-reference doc. | It would go stale; the role files' never-break list and the log's Now block cover it. |

## 2026-10-26 (a second review by another agent)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The free course uses only free content.** Unit 3's song challenge is Ode to Joy's opening, carried as an exercise in the unit's own data; a test checks units 1 to 7. | Ode to Joy became Plus, and the free course must work signed out and offline. |
| **Every song has a public 20 to 30 second preview clip** (a finished mix on the CDN), made in admin with the app's own renderer. The catalogue and song pages carry the clip and each part's name and range, never notes or single parts. | Previews and search pages for Plus songs, without giving the library away. |
| **Landing on `main` checks Hillel's checkout is clean first,** and retries on a lock or a lost race; Hillel edits in his own worktree while agents run. | A fast-forward merge touches that checkout's files. |
| **All 8 templates stay in `web/templates` until M5,** when the Plus songs move to `server/database/templates/`; the schema test covers both folders. | So M1 can check all 8 on both sides. |
| **One trial per card is checked after Checkout,** by cancelling a trial on a card that had one; no-card trials need trust level 2. | Stripe only reveals the card after Checkout. |
| **The real client IP is resolved behind Cloudflare and App Platform** (trusted proxies, with a test). | Otherwise every visitor shares one IP limit. |

## 2026-10-26 (a review by another agent)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Each agent works in its own git worktree from day one** (`agent/backend`, `agent/frontend`), landing small commits on `main` with a rebase and a fast-forward, so history stays linear. The frontend lands stub public views first. Replaces "one shared checkout". | Shared working trees broke the backend's view tests on half-saved files, and raced on the git index. |
| **nginx serves the PWA's files directly** (`try_files`); PHP handles only `/api`, public pages, webhooks and app routes. | With no bundler, dozens of ES module requests per page can't each boot Laravel. |
| **Sample rates are converted, never assumed:** frame counts and offsets are scaled to the AudioContext's rate, including the AAC priming check. | `decodeAudioData` resamples; a 48 kHz guide on a 44.1 kHz device would otherwise misalign. |
| **Wireless mics get a kind warning** in the picker, and Just recorded gets **"Sound off? Nudge timing"** with "Line it up for me". | The spike measured a 44 ms jump on a Continuity mic. |
| **The mic opens during setup and stays open for the session.** | iOS switches its audio session when the mic opens; that shouldn't land on the count-in. |
| **Two queues:** `default` drained each minute; `long` (ffmpeg) on its own resident worker, 2 processes, 300-second timeout. | Renders would otherwise block mail and light jobs. |
| **"Resumable" uploads mean retried from the device's outbox,** not byte-resumed; multipart only if needed. | A presigned PUT is all or nothing. |
| **Takes are stored as 16-bit in IndexedDB from M1** (db.js version 2). | Half the space on phones, before beta singers record. |
| **The first run goes straight into unit 1, lesson 1** with only the mic permission; unit 1 finds the range; the timing check waits for the first timed take. **Wait for me on speakers** plays the note, then goes quiet while you sing. | "Sing within a minute"; and without headphones the tracker would hear the speaker's note. |

## 2026-10-26 (go to market, operations, legal duties)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **No pre-launch waitlist;** the no-bulk-email rule stays as it is. | Hillel's call. The launch is announced through posts, outreach and communities ([go-to-market-plan.md](go-to-market-plan.md)). |
| **Web only,** no app stores for now; the app guides people to install it. | Hillel's call. |
| **People may share and monetise their harmonies,** crediting "Arranged by Stackapella"; the terms and the beta agreement say so. Re-selling the arrangements isn't allowed. | Hillel's call. Every posted video advertises the app. |
| **Opt-in practice reminders by web push, at launch:** offered after setting a weekly goal, at most one a day, kindly worded. Replaces "web push later". Needs `minishlink/web-push` (Hillel's OK). | Hillel's call. Without email, people need a way to be reminded that they chose. |
| **Song pages and guides for search, in the launch;** "How did you hear about us?" and `?ref=` for attribution without analytics. | Search is how beginners look for a part to learn. |
| **Operations:** an Editor role for help with the queues; support targets, saved replies, refunds and account recovery; a cost model at $4.99 (break-even about 40 yearly subscribers); the "only on this device" card; device passes at every milestone; `security.txt`, a security review and a secrets list. | Planned in [operations-plan.md](operations-plan.md); none of it was written down. |
| **Legal duties listed for the lawyer:** the Digital Services Act's statements of reasons and appeals, under-18 defaults, the right of withdrawal and auto-renewal rules at checkout, the European Accessibility Act, cookies. | Added to [trust-and-safety-plan.md](trust-and-safety-plan.md). |

## 2026-10-26 (trust and safety)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Spam and abuse are handled from day one,** planned in [trust-and-safety-plan.md](trust-and-safety-plan.md), with each piece placed in a milestone (M1 to L, then with the network features). | Hillel asked for protection from day one; the earlier plans had only scattered pieces. |
| **Proposed, open to veto:** trust levels 0 to 3 from our own records (verified email, account age, real practice, a paid subscription) set every limit; the limits table, with live overrides in admin; no links in user text at launch; 3 reports hide a shared page; one trial per card fingerprint; a global cap of 2 video renders at once. | Real singers never notice; bots and spammers hit walls. |
| **Proposed: Turnstile is built in M3 and switched on only in "shield mode".** Refines "Turnstile only if abused". | So it's one click during an attack, not a build. |
| **Proposed: Cloudflare in front of the app** (free plan, as iaw). | DDoS and bot protection at the edge. |
| **Proposed: raw IP addresses are kept 30 days in `security_events`, then only a hash;** no device fingerprinting. | Enough to investigate abuse, without tracking. |

## 2026-10-26 (pricing, after a review)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **No licensed music:** only songs public domain everywhere (published 1930 or earlier in the US, and every writer died before 1956 for the UK and EU), and our own originals. | Hillel's call. The rule and the traps are in `docs/templates-plan.md`. |
| **Four songs free** (Amazing Grace, Are You Sleeping, Michael Row the Boat Ashore, Happy Birthday), **everything else Plus,** with **a free song of the month.** Supersedes "all official templates are free". | Hillel's call. A growing library is what learners pay for. |
| **Course work never counts toward the free limit.** | Hillel's call. The free course alone used 2 or 3 songs. |
| ~~**Plus is $59.99 a year or $7.99 a month**~~ **Lowered the same day: a launch price of $4.99 a month or $39.99 a year,** yearly shown first, with a 7-day trial. It rises later for new subscribers; launch subscribers keep their price while subscribed (the founder offer). | Hillel's call: growth at launch first, raise later. |
| **Beta singers don't get Plus as payment;** they're paid their hourly rate instead (decided the same day). | Hillel's call. |
| **Plus songs are served by the API to Plus accounts only;** only the free songs are bundled with the app. | Otherwise the app's files would give the library away. |
| **Proposed, open to veto:** the launch library of about 20 songs, 2 new songs a month after launch, storage of 1 GB free and 50 GB on Plus, the trial's card up front on yearly only. | Made in the review; see the open decisions in `docs/pricing-plan.md`. |

## 2026-10-26 (a course like Simply Piano)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The measure that decides what's next:** finished harmonies and second songs, counted on the server from M2. If they're low, the core is fixed before features are added. | The vision's measure of success, made into a rule. |
| **The singing feedback becomes a gamified course, like Simply Piano:** a course map of units and lessons, lit notes on the Note lane, Wait for me, stars (1 to 3), XP and voice levels, badges, today's workout. The harmony path becomes units 5 to 7. | Hillel's call. |
| **Gentle streaks:** a weekly goal (2, 3 or 5 days), no penalty for a missed week, in the app only. Replaces "no streak pressure". | Hillel's call. |
| **Course units unlock with stars; the library never locks.** | Hillel's call. |
| **Leagues and friends' leaderboards** (when the network features panel opens them, with the gallery): opt-in, ranked by XP (effort), nobody demoted, under-18s see friends only. | Hillel chose leaderboards; this keeps them kind to beginners. |
| **Proposed, open to veto:** units 1 to 7 are free (a first harmony start to finish), later units and unlimited workouts are Plus. | The first finished harmony is what sells Plus. |
| **Music licensing gets an early conversation with the lawyer.** | It's the biggest limit on the library. |

## 2026-10-26 (more features)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Eight more features,** planned in [more-features-plan.md](more-features-plan.md). At launch: **Make it sound good** (levels, gentle compression, a generated room, -14 LUFS), **Your first harmony** (a learning path), **Chords underneath** (a pad or piano backing), and **Share as a video** (a vertical MP4 with karaoke words). Also in the launch: **the harmony gym**. Opened when there's a network: **the open parts shelf**, **the virtual choir**, and **song requests**. | Hillel's call, from the suggestions. |
| **Left out:** gentle pitch correction, gifts, a choir director's progress view (it comes with choirs), licensed pop songs, live singing together, built-in video recording, a general feed, and streaks or reminder emails. | Hillel's call. |
| **Takes are always stored dry,** and polish never touches the timing check, bleed detection or scores. | What's measured must stay true; old harmonies improve when the polish does. |
| **The virtual choir's mix is the one place the server mixes audio.** | A choir mix is new work nobody heard before, so "published audio is what the person heard" doesn't apply. |
| **Share videos are rendered on the server with ffmpeg,** not in the browser. | One look from every browser, and no new JavaScript library. |
| **All eight are free;** the harmony gym follows the warm-ups (one a day free, all on Plus). | They're the core of learning and growth; shared harmonies bring people in. |

## 2026-10-26 (the vision and the roadmap)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Beginners learning harmony come first** at launch: the home screen leads with learning, and their path is polished first. Choir members, creators, teachers and coaches use the same tools. | Hillel's call. Written up in [vision.md](vision.md). |
| **Send to my teacher stays** (Plus): a singer's own teacher reviews in the same workspace with a free account, outside the marketplace's payments. | Hillel's call; it brings teachers in, and some will sell on the marketplace. |
| **One roadmap of milestones (M0 to M10 and launch readiness L, then the network features N1 to N7), each with a backend and a frontend track,** replaces the separate orders of work in the backend plan and the two-agent plan. | So the two agents always share one plan, and each has work from day one. |
| **Laravel serves the PWA, the API and the public pages on one origin;** no separate static site. | The public pages and the app must share cookies. Open to veto. |
| **Records made on the device keep their device UUID** (projects, parts, takes, device templates); server records use Sqids. | Offline creation needs no ID remapping. Open to veto. |
| **The frontend agent owns the public Blade pages' markup** (`server/resources/views/public/`); the backend agent their data. | The pages share the app's design and Tailwind build. |
| **A part's offset is stored in frames** (`offsetFrames`), from db.js version 3; IndexedDB versions are assigned in one registry (`docs/architecture.md`). | Frames are exact; several plans add stores. |
| **"Your singing is analysed on this device"** replaces "Your singing stays on this device". | Takes sync for signed-in people, so the old line would no longer be true. |
| **Whole-flow tests use the `cdp.py` flow scripts,** not Laravel Dusk. | One browser driver, already built and dependency-free. |
| **Proposed, open to veto: an account is never needed to learn or record.** It's asked for only to save to the cloud, share, buy, or at the free limit. The launch library is all 8 templates, each with real voices for every part. | Beginners should be singing within a minute. |

| **Our X and Bluesky accounts post** new templates and featured harmonies (iaw's pattern). **Users share their own** with links and pre-filled posts, without connecting accounts. | Hillel's call. |
| **A public gallery:** harmonies, profiles, likes, comments, follows and reports, plus unlisted links. | Hillel's call (mudeo had the same). |

Coaches are approved by Hillel, then rated after a paid session. | Hillel's call. Replaces "a marketplace only if demand shows up" in the coaching plan. |
| **Live lessons are booked through Event Schedule,** not built in the app, and the two apps integrate more widely (choirs, practice tracks on events, performers). | Hillel's call: ES already has appointments, availability and calendar sync. |

Invoice emails are off by default; paying by invoice is later. **It connects directly, with no need for Event Schedule.** | Hillel's call; ES's integration is the reference. |
| **Song storage: current takes plus a 30-day bin,** which doesn't count toward the allowance. This is the exception to hard deletes. | Hillel's call; storage stays predictable, and Undo works across devices. |
| **Proposed, open to veto:** coaching 18+ at first; 15% commission (30% on exercise packs); coaches get Plus free; Stackapella takes coaching payments and books lessons in ES as already paid; ES becomes an OAuth provider. | Made while planning; see the open decisions in backend-plan.md. |

## 2026-10-26 (two agents at once)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Proposed, open to veto: a backend agent and a frontend agent work at once,** planned in [parallel-agents-plan.md](parallel-agents-plan.md). Each slice's API contract (`/contract`: rules, JSON Schemas, examples) is approved by Hillel first, tested from both sides, and mocked for the frontend. | Hillel asked for two agents building toward an agreed API. |
| **Proposed: two sessions in one checkout on `main`,** each committing only its own folder (enforced by a hook), talking through local session messages; worktrees only as a fallback. | Keeps the straight-to-`main` rule, and Hillel sees and steers both. |
| **Proposed: our own small JSON Schema checker,** not OpenAPI tooling or a package. | No npm, and no new dependency without Hillel's OK (`opis/json-schema` is the candidate if he wants one). |

|---|---|
| **Real singers replace the synthesized guides before launch,** planned in [beta-singers-plan.md](beta-singers-plan.md) (not built yet): Hillel invites singers, reviews their takes in the app, and an accepted take becomes the part's default guide for everyone. | Real voices make templates far more appealing; the beta doubles as early user testing. |
| **Invite links on a minimal backend:** the first slice of `/server` (Laravel, to the conventions), a private link per singer with no password, uploads to Spaces, admin review in the app, on a beta domain. | Hillel's call: in the app, not by email or files. |
| **Recorded guides play only in the original key at 100% speed;** otherwise the synth plays, with a note. No pitch-shifting. | Quality: pitch-shifting a voice sounds worse the further it moves. |
| **Singers are credited by name if they choose,** or stay anonymous. | Hillel's call. |
| ~~**Payment for singers is decided later;** volunteers for now,~~ (**replaced the same day:** singers are paid their hourly rate) under a lawyer-reviewed consent and licence agreement; 18 or over only. | Room left in the data model and agreement. |
| **Recording opens only for reviewed templates,** in one key each (the original), with the words sung. | A take is tied to its template version; a later note fix would orphan it. |
| **Proposed in review, open to veto:** learners can switch each part between the real voice and the synth (real by default); existing projects pick up new real voices; official recorded guides are free for everyone (the Plus row is for people's own templates); one template may mix different singers, judged by ear. | Made while reviewing the plan; Hillel to confirm. |

| **Singing improvement is planned in [vocal-coaching-plan.md](vocal-coaching-plan.md)** (not built yet): pitch accuracy per note, a pitch chart, timing, vibrato, warm-ups in your range, and tips that respond to how you sing. | Gives people a reason to come back every day, and helps the choir market. |
| **Feedback live and after each take.** Live pitch feedback is now in scope (it was listed as later). | Hillel's call: a live line while singing, a report after. |
| **Coaching is built in now; real teachers later** through "Send to my teacher" (a take plus its chart). A marketplace only if demand shows up. **Revised the same day:** a coaching marketplace is in the launch. | Gets most of the value cheaply; a marketplace is a much bigger product. |
| **Basics free, depth on Plus:** the latest report and a daily warm-up are free; history, progress, the full warm-up library, detailed insights and Send to my teacher are Plus. | Hillel's call. |
| **Head vs chest voice is never "detected",** only explained in tips and exercises. Tips and exercises are drafted by Claude and checked by a qualified voice teacher, with no medical claims. | Pitch alone can't tell them apart reliably; voice care advice needs an expert. |

| **Words are pasted and fitted to the notes automatically;** no speech recognition. | Offline, free, every browser, and no audio leaves the device. |

| **Templates are stored on the device for now,** with export and import. The free limit of 1 private template waits for accounts. | Sharing needs the backend; testers need room to make several. |

Cues come from Karaoke stage (1a) and the 2a and 2b desktop layouts. | Hillel's picks from the Claude Design exploration (`docs/design-prompt-explore-learning.md`). |

Learning from public templates is always free. | Free creators grow the public library. The heaviest creators are likely choir directors and teachers, whose needs are private and group ones, which is the clearest paying market. Recorded guides cost storage. Details in `docs/pricing-plan.md`. |

## 2026-10-25 (harmony templates, Phase 1)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **Template projects count like any project** toward the free 3. **Preview** (Listen mode, nothing saved) doesn't count. | Hillel's call; preview keeps templates tryable at the limit. |
| **Claude drafts the official arrangements; a musician checks every part** before release. Drafts carry `reviewed: false` and only show with `?drafts=1`. | Fast to start, quality-checked; the flag is the release gate. |
| **Guides are synthesized from note data** (an "oo" vowel voice, rendered in the browser). **Revised 2026-10-26:** beta singers' voices become the official guides, with the synth as the fallback. | Changes key with no quality loss, small files, no singers needed. |
| **In "Listen, then repeat", the other parts play during your turn**, without your guide, after a one-beat cue. | You answer against the harmony, as when recording. |
| **Phase 1 records whole parts;** re-recording one phrase and live pitch feedback come later. | Punch-in needs crossfades; pitch feedback was already out of scope. |
| The feature plan is [templates-plan.md](templates-plan.md). (The implementation plan was a scratch file outside the repo, since replaced.) | |

## 2026-10-25 (later)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **The frontend is Vue 3, built as an installable PWA. Flutter web is dropped.** | The audio engine is JS anyway. Flutter's runtime adds 2.2 to 3.6 MB gzipped (measured on the spike) plus memory on iOS, where long recordings already use a lot. Real form fields give password-manager autofill and accessibility. People can install it. |
| **No npm at all**: no `package.json`, no `npm install`, no Vite. Libraries load from a CDN, pinned to exact versions with SRI hashes. | Hillel doesn't trust npm (supply-chain attacks, hijacked packages) and never wants to run `npm install`. This is iaw's setup. Unlike iaw and ES, which vendor files, Stackapella uses a CDN (it has no selfhosted version). |
| **Vue compiles templates in the browser** (the ESM build with the compiler), so the CSP allows `'unsafe-eval'`. | It's the only way to write readable templates without a build. To keep it safe, templates live only in our own JS modules, never in the page or data, and user text is never shown through `v-html`. |
| **Tailwind through its standalone binary**, pinned with a sha256, with the built CSS committed. | As iaw does. Tailwind is no npm package here, and deploys need no build. |
| **The spike stays in Flutter** as the test bench until the iOS, Safari and Firefox runs are done. | It already works; only its audio findings carry over. |

## 2026-10-25 (pricing)

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **MP3 export is a Plus feature**, as are stems. WAV export of the mix stays free. | Hillel: the brief isn't binding, and MP3 is a natural reason to upgrade. |
| **Pricing is free plus a paid "Stackapella Plus"**, with the free plan limited to 3 projects. The full plan, its rules and what's still open are in [pricing-plan.md](pricing-plan.md). | From the brief, refined in two reviews. |
| **Account features follow the user; project capacity follows the owner.** All plan checks go through one place. | So a Plus collaborator gets Plus exports anywhere, and a team plan can come later without rewriting every gate. |
| **The MP3 gate is browser-side only**, accepted. | The mix is encoded on the device; every limit that costs money is enforced on the server. |

| Decision | Why |

## 2026-10-25

| Decision | Why |
|---|---|
| **MySQL 8.4**, including tests. DO Managed MySQL in production. | Both Event Schedule and iaw are MySQL only. |
| **Laravel 13, PHP 8.4 in CI.** | The latest stable versions; they match iaw and the local SDK. |
| **Actions for business logic** (`app/Actions/<Domain>/VerbNoun::handle()`), outside APIs behind `app/Services`. | Hillel chose the iaw pattern over Event Schedule's services and fat models. |
| **Sign-in with Google plus email and password** (Sign in with Apple added 2026-10-26). | Reaches singers without a Google account. Needs email verification, password reset and Postmark. |
| ~~Flutter state: plain `ChangeNotifier`, no library.~~ Superseded below: the frontend is Vue. | The iaw `flutter/stats` pattern; no extra dependency. The hard part is the JS audio engine, not app state. |
| **No commit trailers.** "Area: outcome" messages, straight to `main`. | Matches Event Schedule's rule. |
| **iaw is the main style reference**, not `~/Code/itsallwidgets`. | itsallwidgets is the 2018 Laravel 5.6 app that iaw replaces. |
| **The audio engine is our own JS** (Web Audio + AudioWorklet), ~~called from Dart through `dart:js_interop`~~ (the app is now Vue, so it's called directly). | Flutter web has no low-level audio API (from the brief). |
| **The spike lives in `web/spike_audio/`** and is deleted before the MVP. **Revised:** it stays as the test bench until the real-device runs (roadmap M0) are done. | Keeps the repo at two top-level code folders. |
- 2026-10-28, landing agent: the launch kit's images (docs/launch-kit/images) are real screens framed with a caption, made by one script from the guide's shots and the server's pages, so they can be made again when the app changes. Two wait on Hillel: a shared harmony's page (the dev database holds only test data) and Plus (the prices aren't final for the public).
- 2026-10-28, landing agent with the frontend agent: the licence's credit (LICENSE, 2) reads "Stackapella, by Hillel Coren · stackapella.com", linking to https://stackapella.com. On the site it's the foot of every public page, shown once the open_source flag is on and always on a self-hosted install; in the app it's the foot of Settings, under Contact us, shown on a self-hosted install. The name is fixed in code, not the SITE_FOUNDER setting, so an operator can't drop it.
- 2026-10-28, landing agent (the workout, 85.png, handed over by the frontend agent): the warm-up is three rounds a half step apart, run one after another from Start, and its top note leaves room for the last round; other steps stay one round. The lane keeps its glass bricks and states rather than the mockup's purple and orange, by the glass rule. Lower and Higher replace Too high and Too low (the same remembered semitone). The shared Exercise gains a `bare` prop so the workout draws its own Start and starting note; lessons are unchanged.
- 2026-10-28, landing agent (My parts, 91.png, handed over by the frontend agent): the mockup's "Email me when it opens" and "We'll email you" aren't built, by the plan's no-automatic-email rule; a part not open yet says it opens here once it's checked. A submitted or in-review take sits under Coming up (nothing to do yet), and a take not used this time gets its own last section. The credit card's Edit keeps the existing choice (your profile's name or anonymous): the credit's name is the profile's, so there's no separate name to edit. The header keeps Tell Hillel's own words.
- 2026-10-28, landing agent (Hillel: "let people with the beta link try the app without creating an account like normal users will be able to"): on the open beta link and mudeo's early access, Start singing opens the app as a guest with the pass the link sets (the server's comingSoon is false with the pass), and making an account is the second choice. Guests do everything a launch visitor can on the device; what needs an account (sharing a link, groups, Plus, saving to the account) asks at that moment. A friend's song and a choir still ask for an account first, since joining one is an account's.
- 2026-10-28, landing agent (Hillel: "make part of the marketing pitch that you can help fill in the recordings"): the site invites singers to fill in the guide voices, worded to terms 4a and what's built. Most songs start with a practice voice; a harmony is public when its singer says yes to featuring it as they share (18 and over, everyone on it agreeing); we may choose a part as the voice everyone learns from, credited by the profile name; unticking featuring stops it. Written as "we" (Hillel picks the voices himself; no "admins" or "editors"), and as "may": no claim that any song has a singer's voice yet. On the landing (a section after the four steps, with the Share sheet's featuring tick drawn as its picture), /features/real-voices (replacing the gallery-only "Coming" note, which the featuring tick had already overtaken), /features/share, a landing question, and the help guide's Your voice as a guide (which now says to untick featuring to stop, before deleting). The landing's "real singers sing many of the parts" lines now say some, and that most songs start with a practice voice.
- 2026-10-28, landing agent (Hillel, via the coordinator: Harmonise my song's MVP; agreed with the frontend agent): it reuses the creator rather than a new sheet. Harmonise this, in an own song's part panel beside Trim, opens /create?from=<song>&part=<part>&harmonise=1: the take is transcribed at the song's tempo on the creator's grid (from-project.js), the key found, and the Parts step suggests the parts with the creator's styles and Try another chord. Add to my song (stores/projects.js adoptTemplate) gives the song the template, makes the take the tune's part without touching it or its offsets, and adds the others as guide slots after the song's own; the template is a device one (a new UUID, version 1), as renderGuide's cache and sync expect. A take is too unclear when the transcriber finds it empty or noisy, or it has fewer than 4 notes or more than 40% unsure: it gets a kind note with Sing it again and Tap in the notes. Only own songs offer it (a template song already has its parts), and not a beta or friend's song. The plan's tempo map, tap-along beat check and band step wait for later.
- 2026-10-28, landing agent (Hillel, via the coordinator: instrument parts' MVP, on the frontend agent's loader): an instrument part is a template part with `kind: 'instrument'`, as the format already allows; the format has no instrument field and a guide is one whole-pitch note at a time, so the part's key names its sample set (piano, guitar → nylon-guitar, bass-guitar → electric-bass, drums), its name stays free, the piano and guitar play their chords broken into eighths, the bass the roots with a semitone step into the next chord, and the drums a groove as General MIDI notes (36 kick, 38 snare, 42 hat) read as the contract's pieces. js/guides.js renders them through js/instruments.js renderNotes, with `instrument:<set>:<samples|synth>` in the cache key, and falls back to the synth voice (a synthesized kit for the drums, which never transpose). Up to two instruments (six parts in all). partsFor keeps instrument parts whoever sings; the singer counts and the range check leave them out; a part's kind goes up with it so shares can say "played by". Added from the creator's Parts step under suggested parts (so Harmonise this offers them too).

- 2026-10-28, backend agent (Hillel said yes, through the coordinator: carry real voices over): every templates sync moves a recorded voice (a template guide with its beta submission, or an accepted submission not published yet) from an older version to the current one when its part sings the same there: each note's beat, length and pitch, and the song's key and tempo, with words free to differ (`CarryVoicesOver`). It moves rather than copies, because a submission has one guide and a part one default: a project still on the old version then hears the practice voice for that part, which is rare and the version's notes are the same anyway. A moved voice leads on the new version only if nothing leads there yet. Each move is in the audit log (`templates.voices_carried`), and Hillel gets one email per song and version listing each part that had a voice: carried over, or needs re-recording (left out when the new version has its own voice for it). Submissions still in review stay on their version.

- 2026-10-29, backend agent (Hillel, through the coordinator: "Keep people logged in forever, set cookie to remember automatically"): every web sign-in (password, code, link, Google, Apple, Event Schedule) sets Laravel's remember cookie, with no checkbox (`remember: false` on auth.signin is accepted and ignored). It lasts five years (`auth.guards.web.remember`), Secure in production, HttpOnly and SameSite=Lax, and is set again for its full life whenever a visit is restored from it (`RenewRememberCookie`), since Chrome keeps a cookie at most 400 days. A session that expires (240 minutes), or a server restart, never signs anyone out; the web app already refreshes CSRF and retries once on a 419. Signing out ends it on that device only (`logoutCurrentDevice`, no token change, so other devices stay signed in); signing out everywhere, a new password, a suspension and the admin's recover sign-in rotate the token, which ends every remember cookie. The admin console keeps its own idle and maximum life and its second factor per session, and its expiry now signs out only that browser.

- 2026-10-29, backend agent (the leaderboards plan's flag review; the coordinator's queue): a new best is flagged when it's a perfect 100, a jump of 25 or more over the singer's own best on that part, or 15 or more above the global board's best once it has 5 entries (others' held scores don't count toward that best). A flagged score is left off the global board for everyone but its singer for 48 hours, then shows anyway unless an admin took it off; an admin can clear it sooner (audited). Holding rather than hiding means a real great take is only delayed, and Hillel's attention is asked for, not required. Friends' boards aren't held: they're people who know each other.

- 2026-10-29, backend agent (Hillel, through the coordinator, on the leaderboards' later items: "Go on with them"): "report this name" is built. A signed-in viewer reports someone else's board entry (`reports.create` `boardEntryId`); reports from 2 different people hold it off the global board until an admin looks, however long (unlike a flag's 48-hour hold, since a name can hurt), and the flag list shows it as `reported`. Clearing it dismisses the reports; taking it off upholds them. Agreed with the landing agent (the Report action on the board) and the frontend (the admin list). The friend-beat-you bell and earlier versions' boards follow.

And CI gains a `secrets` job: gitleaks 8.30.1 (pinned, checked against its published sha256) running server/tool/leak-check.sh over the whole history and the published tree, the same check as before every push.

It no longer waits for a domain to be onboarded (`MAIL_MUDEO_READY` unset means ready, false holds it back); it still needs the postal address. The batches of 50, the daily cap and the stop on a high bounce rate keep an old list's bounces from weighing on the account emails, which share the domain now.

Before a line is sung the key is the draft's C; after, it's the key found in what you sang, or the one you chose. It's an offer, never a step.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel: "on the check page offer to auto fix notes"): the builder's Check page lists notes worth a listen (ones the transcriber wasn't sure of, and octave slips), not harmony problems, so the fixes are for those. A safe fix exists for two kinds only (`fixFor` in js/creator.js): an octave slip moves an octave toward its neighbours, and a note outside the key moves to the nearest note in it (toward the next note on a tie; a minor key's raised seventh counts as in it). A note in the key with close neighbours has no fix: only an ear can say, so it keeps Hear it, Higher, Lower and Keep it. The banner offers "Fix the N we can", each such note "Fix it: A4" with the reason, and after either a card says what changed ("Fixed 1 note: B5 → B4 (ing)") with Undo, which puts the sung lines back as they were. An edit by hand drops the Undo.

Every song card on Home, yours and the library's, has its preview Play at the picture's top right, on hover on a desktop and always on touch; it only plays (a tap on the card still opens it), one thing at a time. A library card's tags sit at its top left. Library cards read the catalogue's `voicings` for "2 to 4".

A minor whose relative major isn't in the progression stays as it was, so the stripes always point at a chord you can see.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel, on the chord cards: "find a graphical way to represent the 6 main chords in a key ... HOME, the 4 and 5 ... and all relative minors"; "remove shapes/number/letters and instead combine shapes and numbers into one style. remove text underneath"; "Use roman numeral b/c they can show major/minor"): one style, no Show as control, no text under the cards. Each chord is its family's shape with its Roman numeral under it (I IV V upper case, ii iii vi lower, ♭VII, V7). The key's six chords are three families in the circle's order: IV and ii (Lift), I and vi (Home), V and iii (Pull back home); a major is a solid glass shape in its family's colour, its relative minor the same shape hollow and a little lower; a minor key counts from its relative major (Am in A minor is Home's hollow shape); a chord outside the six is a grey dashed outline. Supersedes this morning's striped relative minors and "Am is C's relative minor" lines.

Your songs' head says "1 of 3 free songs used" over a three-step meter, with Get Plus (not on Plus); New song is "+ New song" in a dashed card with "Start blank or from a library song" under it. Under each shape, its name: Letters (default) in C, and a minor progression in A minor, C's relative, so every card spells the same six chords (C Dm Em F G Am); Numbers from the progression's own tonic (1 4 5 6m, 1m ♭7 ♭6 5, 57 for a seventh); or Numerals (I IV V vi, ♭VII). A small Letters | Numbers | Numerals toggle above the cards is kept on the device (prefs.chordLabels).
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel: "where are the posts to review?"): Admin > Posts (admins) lists our X and Bluesky posts, waiting for approval first, with filters Waiting, Approved, Sent, Failed, Cancelled and All. Each shows the network and occasion, the song or harmony it's about (linked through the post's own link), the text as it will go out, and its status; Approve sends it in 2 minutes (Stop it undoes it until then), Cancel asks first in a styled sheet, and Send again retries a failed one. The nav shows how many wait ("20+" past a page). Posts carry no media in the contract, so there's no media preview.
- 2026-10-29, courses-skills (delegated; the lead and the coordinator agreed the order): Perfect your pitch is built first, then Breath and stamina. Its outline is docs/courses/perfect-pitch.md, after a voice-teacher and a game-design review. It changes the content doc's plan in two places: the free lesson is Bullseye (echo a note, see where it lands as a dart), with "hear a note through a distraction" moved to unit 4; and "naming a note cold" became "Hold your ground" (holding a note against moving parts), since naming notes belongs to Ear training and Sight-singing. Lock to a drone is starred only on do and so (a drone-tuned third sits 14 cents under equal temperament, which the app scores against), and a note from memory earns three stars within a semitone (Levitin: most people are within two). The measures are web/js/pitch-skills.js (landing, lock, centre, drift, memory), shared with courses-styles for scoops and fall-offs.
- 2026-10-29, courses-skills (the coordinator and the lead agreed): Singing in another language doesn't use O Sole Mio, which isn't safely public domain (an Italian court added Alfredo Mazzucchi, who died in 1972, as a co-author). A clearly public-domain chorus replaces it (Santa Lucia or Funiculì, Funiculà), chosen with its outline.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel: "lets change to a more modern flat style instead. the blocks will stand out even more"): every button is flat, changed once in input.css's shared classes (btn-primary, btn-secondary, btn-danger, glass-record, glass-bubble, glass-bubble-solid, which every chip, segmented control, icon button and the transport's Play and Record use): a solid fill or a clean outline, no gloss, rim, inner shine or shadow, a tint on hover and a little more on press, the focus ring every control has. Shapes stay as Hillel's own designs draw them (pills for buttons, circles for icon buttons). The note gems and bricks are now the only glossy things. Two one-off glossy buttons (the take result's Play, the song cards' shadowed Play) went flat too.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel's Home workout-card design and notes): the course card, Today's workout and Your songs share one width; on a wide screen the week (ring, days, level) sits inside the course card on its right, and Install drops below it at the same width. The course card has the lesson's label and title, the unit's progress as a bar with "N lessons left in this unit", and a normal-size Continue lesson. The workout's steps are numbered, each with its own length, a longer step a wider tile, and "Start workout" sits in a footer row inside the card beside "3 exercises · no setup needed". Grey secondary text is lighter in the Warm, Midnight and Graphite themes (muted a quarter of the way to the text colour: about 7:1 on raised surfaces, up from about 5.4); Stage already had 9:1 or more and keeps its grey.
- 2026-10-29, courses-styles (delegated, after a voice-teacher and a game-design review): Folk and early music is the styles agent's first course (it runs on what exists: drones, public-domain tunes as steps, held-note scoring), outlined in docs/courses/folk.md: four units (the plain voice, old scales, ornaments, on your own), 16 lessons. Scarborough Fair is dropped for lesson 1, because the tune everyone knows reached print through a 1960s collector's book ("when in doubt, pick another"); lesson 1 is Drunken Sailor, a Dorian shanty documented from the 1830s, and Greensleeves is dropped too (its mode depends on the version). A plain tone is scored as a steady hold, never as straightness, so a singer with a natural vibrato never clamps the throat to kill it. Unaccompanied lines are scored on key drift and intervals alone (`keyDrift` in web/js/style-scores.js: a robust line fit through the notes' centres, short notes left out, 60% intervals and 40% the key held), and the drone comes back only after the last note. Loudness is never scored or warned from the level (auto gain flattens it): "Strong, not loud" is said in words.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel, on the song builder's note editor: "if I press play then stop I can't press play again"; "if I change from lane to staff the draw select options don't seem to work"): (1) the cause was Vue's reactivity: the editor kept its player in data, so `this.player` read back as a reactive copy and `this.player === player` was never true, and the player was never cleared after Stop (or after the song ended), so the next press stopped it again. Players are kept raw (markRaw) now, in the editor, the Parts step and a song's page, which had the same pattern. (2) The staff was a picture only. It's editable now: Draw places a note where you press (the staff line's pitch in the key, on the grid; staffPitch in js/sing.js, the inverse of staffLayout's position, with a round-trip test), Select picks the note pressed, and the arrows and Delete work on it as on the lane. The tool chosen stays across Lane and Staff, and the staff leaves two beats of room after the line to draw into.
- 2026-10-29, courses-skills (delegated, after a voice-teacher and a game-design review): Breath and stamina never stars length. A learner's first hold sets their own baseline; later goals are that plus 2, 4 or 6 seconds, never under 8 or over 15, and longer holds are "You just did that" moments only (normal maximum phonation time is 15 to 25 seconds at full effort, less with age, so fixed goals would star lung size). Stopping while still steady is the win. No result calls a start "clean", since the pitch tracker can't tell a hard or breathy start from a balanced one. The outline is docs/courses/breath.md.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel, on the note editor: "I'd like to be able to manage the playhead better"; "the play button wraps to the next line, lets move + - to the bottom right under the notes grid to save space"; "also clean up split, join and delete"): the lane's playhead is always there and is where Play starts. Drag its handle (pointer capture; it grows when the pointer's near), press the ruler to put it there (on the grid), or, with the handle focused, the arrows move it a beat (Shift: a bar), Home to the start and End to the end; Back to the start sits by Play, and Home works anywhere in the editor. Moving it while playing plays on from there; picking a note puts it at the note, as Play from the selected note did before. It's one component (components/playhead.js) for the studio's playhead to share. Zoom − and + moved under the notes at the bottom right, so the toolbar keeps to one line. Split, Join and Delete are one group of flat buttons with icons, each with its key in its tooltip (S, J, Delete; S and J are new keys), disabled with the reason said under them ("Too short to split at this snap.", "There's no note after it to join."); a phone's note sheet has the same row.
- 2026-10-29, courses-harmony (delegated; the lead and the coordinator agreed the order): Counterpoint is the harmony agent's first course, then Improvising a harmony, then Barbershop and tags. Its outline, after a voice-teacher and a game-design review, is docs/courses/counterpoint.md: 5 units, 22 lessons. Lesson 1 is a Row, Row round where the voices join during your own take, with an encore of your take as all four voices. Every learner line sits within a fourth above and a major sixth below the comfortable note, each tune placed in its own key to fit. The partner songs are Row, Row with Three Blind Mice (both 6/8), not Are You Sleeping (two against three). A note counts as "pulled" only onto a partner's note within a major third that isn't your own previous or next note, and never on a speaker. A lit fifth or octave dims live when it would make a parallel, so a lit note never costs a star. Improvising a harmony's free lesson uses Michael, Row the Boat Ashore, not You Are My Sunshine (1939, in copyright). The measures are a pure web/js/harmony.js.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel's warm-up design for Today's workout, step 1, and his notes): "Step 1 of 3" in the header (no minutes). The rounds are tabs over the notes, each with its starting note ("Starts on C#3"), ticked when sung, and a tap hears that round. The lane is labelled: each row's note down the side with a line through it, no drifting specks, room so no brick is cut off, and a turn drawn once with the playhead crossing it twice (listening, then singing back), so the whole pattern fits, a phone's too. Each round starts with two beats of rest on the audio clock, "Get ready" before round 1 and "Breathe" between rounds, with "Round 2 of 3" under the lane. Hear it first hides while you sing. The starting note is a compact − and + (held while a round plays). The footer follows the content rather than sticking to the bottom.

- 2026-10-29, courses-styles (delegated, after a voice-teacher and a game-design review): Yodeling (docs/courses/yodel.md) is four units, 16 lessons, on our own yodel refrains and two public-domain tags (Home on the Range, Red River Valley). It doesn't ship until every exercise can be set from the singer's own flip note (lesson 1's siren up and down, the median of the downward jumps, kept as `progress.flip`, and an exercise `base: 'flip'`), because with the top a fourth above the comfortable note most men would earn stars for a chest-voice jump. The flip is measured as clean, nearly or slid (`flip` in web/js/style-scores.js), up and down apart, never as milliseconds to beat (they'd reward a slammed jerk); stars are half flips, half pitch, and the self-check ("a different voice?") steers the advice because the app hears the jump, not the register.
- 2026-10-29, courses-styles (delegated, after a voice-teacher and a game-design review): Pop and country styling (docs/courses/pop-country.md) is four units, 16 lessons, on Down in the Valley, Bury Me Not on the Lone Prairie (Lomax 1910 words, The Ocean Burial tune of 1839), Aura Lee (Fosdick's 1861 words only) and Beautiful Dreamer's first line. Its moves (scoop, fall-off, slide, cry) are measured by shape on notes of half a second or more; a styled scoop must start a semitone or more below and rise smoothly, so an accidental flat start never earns style stars. Tone colour (breathy, bright, twang) is self-checked only, each colour riding on a move we can measure, and the course stays "soon" until real-singer clips can model the colours (the guide's one "oo" voice can't). Where the learner chooses the moves, stars come from pitch and any one move heard, never more for more.

- 2026-10-29, courses-harmony (delegated): Counterpoint's minor round is Are You Sleeping in minor (mi and la lowered, as Mahler set it in his First Symphony), not Hey, Ho, Nobody Home, and its canon at the octave is an original line, not Tallis's Canon. Neither Hey, Ho nor Tallis could be transcribed from a checked full source today, and a wrong transcription would teach the wrong tune; the minor Are You Sleeping also teaches "same shape, darker" more clearly, straight after the major one. Either can come back once transcribed from Pammelia (1609) or Parker's Psalter (1567).
- 2026-10-29, backend agent (Hillel approved "Yes" to local-only firsts and an A/B variant on the device; the API agreed with the frontend and the landing agent): the funnel rides the anonymous counts. `counts.create` takes the `f_*` firsts and `ask_<name>_shown`/`_yes` kinds (config/counts.php `funnel` and `asks`) and an optional `variant`, "<experiment>-<arm>"; the server keeps a variant only on those kinds and only when config/counts.php names that experiment's arm, and a row with a variant keeps no country (usage_counts gains `variant`, '' for none). Experiments live in config/counts.php, one at a time (id, arms, start, end, plannedPerArm, primary, guardrail): `auth.config.experiment` gives `{ id, arms, enrol }`, enrolment stopping by itself once every arm has its planned arrivals (or at its end), and the experiment leaving auth.config a week later. Arms are drawn at random on the device (the landing draws in the browser for its Start links; `?v=` is an uncounted preview only). `admin.funnel.show` gives each step's totals and rate per arm against arrivals (f_arrive plus f_arrive_start), the two arms' difference with a 95% interval, a 50/50 split check (p below 0.001 flags a bug), each ask's acceptance, and daily totals; without an experiment, every device over 7, 30 or 90 days. An unknown kind is dropped on its own, never failing the batch.

Ringing is judged by its own measure, not ±20 cents: a held chord note's centre against its pure target (third -14, seventh -31, the fifth where the piano puts it, all against the chord's root as the guides sing it), 3 stars within 6 cents and never for the piano's third. - 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hillel's "Yes" to local-only firsts and an A/B variant on the device; docs/onboarding-review.md section 4): the app counts the onboarding funnel through js/funnel.js. Each step is a "first", counted once per device: the device keeps only which firsts it has counted, the day it arrived and its experiment arm (localStorage `stackapella.funnel`), and none of it leaves except inside the daily totals. The first open counts `f_arrive` (or `f_arrive_start` with `?from=start`) and, while `auth.config.experiment` enrols, draws the arm with `crypto.getRandomValues`; an open the next day counts `f_day2`, days 1 to 6 `f_week1`, and a weekly goal met within them `f_goal_met`. The steps are counted where they happen: the mic opening, the home note found or skipped, lessons 1 to 3 and unit 1 of the free course (by lessons with stars), the goal chosen, installed (the event, an accepted prompt or the first standalone open), signed in, a name, a share, reminders on, a first take, a take that makes two sung parts. Asks count shown and yes once each: install (the card shown), goal and Remind me (the course hub), with account, name and share to come with onboarding items 5 to 7, and `f_chord` with the chord moment. A landing variant (`?v=land1-b`) is kept for the visit only (sessionStorage), so the site's Start compares with the app's first take; the server drops a variant it doesn't name. Every batch carries the variant (counts.create `variant`). Admin > Funnel (admin.funnel.show) shows every device over 7, 30 or 90 days, or one experiment's arms side by side: rates of arrivals, B minus A in points with its 95% interval (green or red only when the interval leaves out 0), the primary and guardrail steps marked, a red notice when the arms don't split 50/50 (a bug, not a result), and a warning until there are enough arrivals and a week after enrolment stops. The day-by-day totals the report carries aren't drawn yet.

- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (a beta singer via Hillel: "the mute and solo buttons in the studio do not work reliably"): two causes, both in the beta guide studio, where the part being recorded keeps the singer's practice focus. The mix's focus path never read Solo, so a solo dimmed the other rows but every part still played. And Sing's "listen alone" (only the melody under your part) carried into the studio, so with nothing muted only the melody and the singer's part played while every row looked on, which made Mute and unmute on the others seem to do nothing. Now every path of the mix (js/practice.js voiceLevels) honours Solo then Mute (Sing turns solos off for its visit, so Sing is unchanged), and a beta studio plays every other row as its buttons say; the recorded part's own guide still follows its practice. Solo and Mute taps are saved at once (sliders still save once they rest), so a reload straight after keeps them. tests/practice.test.js and flows/studio_solo.py cover it. Also: the mock's beta Amazing Grace is template version 3 (the template moved to 3 with the "straight or free" change), which had left the mock beta studio unable to open.
- 2026-10-29, backend agent (Hillel approved docs/repos-plan.md and created hillelcoren/stackapella-courses, public; "Keep the history"): the courses and songs move to their own public repo with their git history (git filter-branch on a fresh clone: web/course to course/, web/templates to templates/free/, server/database/templates to templates/library/). Main pins one commit in .do/content.lock (commit and its tarball's sha256); the Dockerfile and CI fetch exactly that into today's paths (server/tool/content.sh fetch), and a checkout links its own (content.sh link, CONTENT_DIR for an agent's working copy). Agents don't push: the coordinator runs each push. The split is deterministic, so it's pushed early and again at the switch (a fast-forward), keeping the freeze to the switch itself.
- 2026-10-29, arranger (the coordinator, from a beta singer's "in the tones, there is no place for breath"): every library part was audited for breath marks (lines measured `br` to `br`, in seconds at tempo). Lines over 12 s get a `br` where the words breathe: Let Me Call You Sweetheart at 9, 33, 57 and 81 in all parts; Steal Away at 8, 39 and 72, with its hummed verse given breaths of its own; Sweet Adeline at "Sweet Adeline" (28); the bass hums of Shenandoah (9) and Down to the River (60). Keep on the Sunny Side's phrase list now starts on its `br` notes. Silent Night's last line (13.6 s, mostly the held "peace") stays one breath, as sung. Only `br`, hummed "oo" lyrics and phrase lists change, never a note's beat, length or pitch, so each song's version goes up (the sync's music hash covers them) and every recorded voice carries over (CarryVoicesOver compares notes only). The guide tones' audible breath and the lane's breath mark in the studio and the beta guide studio are the frontend's.
- 2026-10-29, courses-styles (the lead's quality review): Yodeling's siren is three short ones, the third up to 17 semitones above the comfortable note, because most men's flips sit 14 to 17 above it and the old siren (to 12) would have left them on a fallback that rewards a chest-voice jump. A flip is kept only when two downward flips agree within 2 semitones, 3 to 15 above, with voice reached above it; until one is, steps set from it star pitch only and say why. Pop's moves were re-measured against the guide's own model and plain singing: a copy of the guide always counts, plain legato never does.

- 2026-10-29, arranger (delegated, after a barbershop review), on Down by the Old Mill Stream: the chorus only, in B-flat at men's pitch, true barbershop (tenor, lead with the tune, baritone, bass; voicings 2 = lead and baritone, 3 adds the bass). Tune, words and chords come from the 1910 first edition (Tell Taylor, Chicago) and its piano part; the voicing is our own, written over them. The edition's male quartet (arranged by Chas. Miller, whose dates are unknown) is used only to check the chords, never copied, so the EU term can't catch it. The 1910 echoes "(sweet six-teen)" and "(vil-lage queen)" are kept, the lead singing "You were six-teen" and "my vil-lage queen" alone. Accepted: a bare fourth for lead and baritone on "mill" (the tenor has the third) and one passing beat in "blue".

- 2026-10-29, arranger (delegated, each after a choral review; committed in the courses repo): the next three months' songs, all `reviewed: false`.
  - Blow the Man Down, from Whall's *Ships, Sea Songs and Shanties* (1913): the soprano sings the shantyman's calls alone, and all four sing the crew's answers. Kept as Whall prints it: "Way-ay", not today's "Way, hey". No hum under the calls (the reviewer: the lone shantyman is the point of a shanty).
  - Loch Lomond, from *Songs of the North* (1885), in G: the tenor answers the tune's "high road" and "low road" phrases in the chorus, and the bass keeps a drone.
  - Now Is the Month of Maying, Morley's own five voices from Fellowes's 1913 edition: his two tenors are joined into one, using the Quintus and taking his Tenor's notes where those carry the third or root. One change to his Altus gives the duet the leading tone at "greeny grass". AABB on verse 1, as the 1595 part-books write it. The template check's advice there is Morley's two-voice style (unison closes, bare fifths), kept as written.

- 2026-10-29, backend agent (Hillel: "when recording over a track add an undo option to go back to the last take if it was better. Maybe keep all takes so users can swap to try. You'd see the takes by expanding the row"; agreed with frontend2): a part keeps every take. A replaced take is kept, never binned, counting toward storage until it's deleted; parts.takes.list lists a part's kept takes (newest first, with each one's pitch score and who sang it on a shared song), parts.takes.use makes one current again whole (the nudge and trims back to 0), and takes.delete sends one to Recently deleted (never the current one). The purge no longer bins a take no part plays: every take is listed and deletable, so none uses storage unseen. takes.create's storage check still counts the current take as gone, so re-recording at the limit works; the kept take then counts, which can only stop the next new take.
- 2026-10-29, frontend2 (Hillel's studio asks, handed over by the frontend): Record in the studio always starts at 0:00 after the count-in, wherever the playhead was ("In the studio we should always rewind to the start before recording"); Sing still records from where it is. A new track is "Track 1", "Track 2" and so on ("don't auto name tracks melody, harmony, etc. Just name the Track 1 by default and enable the user to rename the tracks"), renamed in its open row (Enter saves, Escape keeps the old name); a song's own parts keep their names and can be renamed too. The first track sets a free song's length ("the first track should set the length of the song. when recording future tracks recording should stop (and playhead reset) when reaching the end of the first track"): a later take stops by itself just after it, is kept, and the playhead goes back to 0:00; the first track is the earliest with a take other than the one being recorded over, so clearing it lets the next take set the length; a song's template sets it for a song.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hum a tune's screens, docs/hum-a-tune-plan.md; Hillel: free time with a click optional, "mm", "32 bars or a minute"): New song offers "Hum a tune" (staff only until the server switches it on with `humTune`), a builder mode of four steps: Hum it, Notes (the note editor, which shows the notes it wasn't sure of), Parts, Save. Hum it records in your own time up to a minute, the pitch drawn live; Done writes it down with the arranger's `noteEvents` and `humToMelody` ("16 notes at 80 BPM in 3/4, key of D"), a low hum placed an octave up for the tune's part, and Play it plays the tune by the guide voice. When the beat or the bars aren't sure, it asks once: Tap along while your hum plays back (a button, or Space), and the bars follow the taps (`beatsFromTaps`). The take stays in memory; the notes are what's kept. Still to come: "Use a click" while humming, Again for one line (`rehum`), and sharing the tune as an idea. The note roll names its pickup bar only where it fits.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hum a tune, docs/hum-a-tune-plan.md): once a tune is written down, each of its lines has "Line 2 again": hum just that line, and it takes its place on the tune's beat grid with the tempo, bars and key kept (the arranger's `rehum`), in the tune's octave.
- 2026-10-29, frontend agent (Hum a tune, Hillel's "free time, click optional"): before humming, "Use a click" (off by default) with its beats a minute (50 to 160): a minute of ticks on the audio clock under the take, the bar's first a higher tick, and their times are the beat, so only the metre and the bars are found and Tap along isn't needed. Through a speaker the mic hears the ticks too: anything that high and short (above D6, under 0.15 s) is never taken for a hummed note.
- 2026-10-29, courses-skills (Hillel chose idea 5 of docs/courses-ideas.md, through the coordinator; hooks agreed with the frontend agent): Sharpen up replays a Plus course's weakest lessons. "Weakest" is the kept result: fewest stars, then lowest best percent, then longest ago, because stars are what both skins keep and the percent breaks ties fairly. It offers two lessons at three done and three from five, never the whole course. Each lesson starts at its last scored step (or the earlier step it sings over or compares with), so it's practice, not a re-read of the Learn cards. It plays through the ordinary lesson view, so each lesson keeps its own best and nothing new is stored. The queue lives in this tab's session storage only. It's for Plus courses only; the free course already has its workout's weak spot.

All 58 facts moved out of the "Scored on" lines (by text edit for hand-written courses; the Counterpoint and Improvising generators call tools/facts.py with tools/fact_sources.json). Both course checks now fail on a malformed fact, an id used twice, more than two in a unit, a "Did you know" anywhere but a fact, and two courses' facts sharing two names or years (unless listed in distinct). The extras (fact, and harmony's keepSong for Keep the finale) don't make a lesson web-only: an app may leave them out. Takes recorded on another device show from the account's list and switch there. Kept takes count toward storage on both sides.

The kept take is new on-device storage: the raw voice (16-bit) and when it was sung, in IndexedDB under `course:<id>` (one per course, a few seconds to about 20, so well under 2 MB each), never synced, never sent, nothing on the server, and never replaced by a later take. The course check now makes sure each `thenAndNow` names a take an earlier lesson of the course keeps.
